
## Knowledge references (`References.bin`)

`aomac::play::references` follows installed `references.dll` (MD5
`4ae74e444c445fbc9a63745a85e40bd0`), `RefMan_t::Save` **0x10001784** and
`Load` **0x10001d50**. All scalar words are **big endian**: BinaryIO.dll
`File_t::WriteU32` **0x10003741** and `ReadU32` **0x10003267**, byte-order
argument zero, explicitly write/read most significant byte first.

Header: `u32 version=1003`, `u32 record_count`. Each record starts with a
`u32 type`: **1** is `u32 low_template, high_template, QL`; **0** is
`u32 original_identity_type, original_identity_id, payload_byte_length`,
followed by exactly that many raw bytes, with no terminating NUL. Character
payload is captured information HTML, not a plain character name. Native
load rejects lengths above **0x7c00**, truncates the resulting string at its
first embedded NUL, and reads at most the configured maximum record count.
The parser reports malformed/truncated input instead of publishing a partial
store; unknown tags are rejected. Neither runtime dummy-item identities,
map keys, nor grid positions are stored here. Keys regenerate from one in
load order; item triples deduplicate and character identities deduplicate
while updating the captured payload (`AddItemRef` **0x10001a87**,
`AddCharRef` **0x10001bf3**). Zero item fields and all-zero character
identities do not create records.

`Initialise` **0x100020c3**/constructor **0x10001f7d** establish the directory,
maximum load count and empty map; loading is explicit. New additions save
when the caller requests it, deletion (**0x100019be**, **0x100018bb**) saves
before its notification, and shutdown **0x100020a1** invokes destructor
**0x1000200b**, which saves before clearing. Reinitialization also destroys
and saves the prior singleton. The Rust caller owns this lifecycle and
character directory; `save` uses the native **0600** file permissions.
`GetCharName` **0x100013b3** extracts the HTML payload before the first LF,
then applies Utils `StripHTML` and `ConvertCharRefs`; `IsCharacter`
**0x1000104b** tests the original identity's type field for nonzero.
Regression `native_bytes_keys_dedup_and_malformed` covers exact bytes,
rekeying, deduplication, limits, truncation, storage and permissions.

## installer

Ported from the decompiled PRK launcher (`MainWindow.DownloadLatestVersion`, `HttpPatchServerService`, `VersionMap`, `VersionIdentifier`, `DeletionHandler`, `RDBController.ApplyRDBPatch`). Implementation: `crates/ao-install`.

- **Map**: `GET https://patches.project-rk.com/patches/patches.map`, lines `src = dst` (split on `=`; lines not giving exactly 2 parts are ignored). Many sources map to `0.3.0`/`0.5.0`; the main chain is `0.0.0=0.6.1`, `0.6.1=0.6.2` … `0.6.36=0.7.0`, `0.7.0=0.7.1`, `0.7.1=0.7.2`.
- **Version** (`VersionIdentifier`): `M.m.p[-pre][+build]`, equality is field-wise (pre-release parts after the first `-` are concatenated). Current version = `patch.version` in the client root, else `0.0.0`.
- **Loop**: while a patch with `src == current` exists: download `patches/<dst>.zip` → extract over the client dir with overwrite → if `<dst>.rdbpatch` in client root: apply and delete it → delete zip → if `<dst>.df` in client root: process it (file stays) → write `patch.version = dst` (tmp file + rename). Any failure before that leaves `patch.version` untouched: extraction overwrites and the rdbpatch is one `INSERT OR REPLACE` transaction, so a rerun redoes the step; an unreadable zip is deleted so it is re-downloaded. Unit test `failed_patch_keeps_version` injects a corrupt zip, a zip-slip entry and a truncated rdbpatch.
- **Zip**: paths relative to client root (e.g. `cd_image/...`); we reject entries that escape it (zip-slip). Resume: partial zip is kept in `<client>/../downloads/<dst>.zip`, continued with HTTP `Range`. Verified live: `0.7.0.zip` (140 733 024 B) killed at 25 173 681 B, rerun sent `Range: bytes=25173681-` → server `206`; result sha256 `22a5f7e7…edf1de` equals a fresh `curl` download (server sends `Accept-Ranges: bytes`, `ETag`). A 416 is only trusted if a HEAD `Content-Length` equals the local size (else the stale file is deleted and refetched); a 206 is only appended if `Content-Range` starts at the local size (else restart from 0). `apply_rdbpatch` opens `rdb.db` without CREATE (missing db = error); `patch.version` temp file and the client dir are fsynced around the rename. Covered by unit tests with a local HTTP server.
- **`.rdbpatch`**: `i32 count`, then per record `u32 type, u32 instance, u32 version, i32 len, bytes[len]` (little endian). The launcher ignores the per-record version and runs `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO [rdb_<type>] (id, version, data) VALUES (<instance>, 1, <bytes>)` on `cd_image/rdb.db`. We do it in one transaction (missing tables are created with the same schema).
- **`.df`**: one client-relative path per line; existing files are deleted, then emptied parent directories (never the client root) are removed. We reject absolute / `..` entries. Backslashes are treated as separators (the shipped `0.6.3.df` is `cd_image\twk\Tweak_….txt`; the launcher only maps `/`, so on non-Windows it would never match). Of all 50 zips in patches.map (central directories read by ranged GET) only `0.6.3.zip` contains a `.df`; `.rdbpatch` is in 0.5.1–0.5.3 and 0.6.2–0.6.36. Scratch run `0.6.2→0.7.2` in `/tmp/aoi-scratch`: the listed file was deleted, sibling kept, `0.6.3.df` left in place and every `.rdbpatch` removed — identical to the launcher (`RDBController.ApplyRDBPatch(deleteWhenDone=true)` deletes the rdbpatch; `MainWindow` never deletes the `.df`).

## renderer

`ao-render` consumes `ao_scene::Scene` only (no format knowledge). wgpu 24 / Metal, winit 0.30.

- GPU layout: per mesh one vertex buffer (48 B: pos, normal, uv, linear RGBA colour) + one u32 index buffer; instances (mat4, step mode Instance) live in a 3-buffer ring rewritten every frame with the frustum-visible instances, grouped by mesh, so one `draw_indexed` per submesh covers all visible instances of that mesh. Per (texture, material) one bind group (texture, sampler, 80 B uniform). Textures: RGBA8/BC1/BC2 UNORM (encoded gamma bytes, no sRGB decode), CPU box-filter mip chain, **Repeat** addressing, linear mip filter, 16x anisotropy (one shared sampler, so terrain textures included); submeshes without a texture (or with an invalid one) use 1x1 white. 4x MSAA, Depth32Float.
- Pipelines: 4 blend modes x {culled, two-sided} = 8. Front = CCW, back faces culled unless `two_sided`. Opaque / AlphaTest (`discard` at alpha < 0.5, depth write) draw first, sorted by pipeline/mesh/material. AlphaBlend (src-alpha, no depth write, constant -2 / slope -2 depth bias so coplanar overlays win against the opaque base) and Additive (src-alpha x dst-one, glow fades out with fog rather than tinting) draw afterwards as one draw per (visible instance, submesh), sorted far-to-near by instance bounding-sphere centre; ties (submeshes of one instance) keep submesh order. Fragment = D3D7 fixed function in the client's gamma space (texel x tint x Gouraud vertex colour + specular, see *Vertex lighting*; no normal flip: D3D has no two-sided lighting); fog is linear (`fog_start`..`fog_end`) toward `fog_color`, mixed in gamma space. Clear colour = `Environment::sky_color`; far plane = 1.1 x `fog_end`. `Environment` None -> defaults (sky sRGB 0.53/0.72/0.92, fog start/end = 0.5/1.0 x max(2 x scene radius, 300 m), ambient 0.35/0.38/0.45, sun 0.75/0.71/0.64 from (0.4, 0.8, 0.3), sun specular intensity 1.0).
- **Gamma-space framebuffer and texture filtering**: world, actors, sky, GUI, clears and MSAA resolve use UNORM (offscreen `Rgba8Unorm`, surface's non-sRGB format). World/env shaders write their gamma-space lighting result directly; sky output and clear colours receive the encoding previously performed by the sRGB target, preserving their unblended shading. Native sampled images use `Rgba8Unorm` / `Bc1RgbaUnorm` / `Bc2RgbaUnorm`, so interpolation operates on encoded texels directly, not on sRGB-decoded texels. No post-sample sRGB encoding remains in world/actor, sky, environment, hologram or terrain shaders. Evidence: Randy `TextureStreamCreator::CreateTexture` `100193e4` selects ordinary RGB565/RGB888/RGBA4444/RGBA8888/DXT1/DXT3 and calls `render_t::D3DXLoadTextureFromMemory`; the D3D7 fixed-function texture path has no sRGB sampling state. DisplaySystem Sprite3 `100283ec` and Cylinder `100109a4` program texture-stage MIN/MAG/MIP states 16/17/18 to POINT when requested, without a colour transform. This closes the former sRGB-decode-before-filter mismatch without manual shader taps. Native effect pipelines appended at 22..30 preserve existing pipeline indices: Sprite3 states SRCBLEND(19)/DESTBLEND(20) = **1/3** (`ZERO/SRCCOLOR`) or **9/3** (`DSTCOLOR/SRCCOLOR`), Cylinder = **2/6** (`ONE/INVSRCALPHA`), sky `HorizonCloudsRim` (`Tweak_Rubi-Ka_HorizonClouds.txt`) = **3/2** (`SRCCOLOR/ONE`, `Blend::SourceColorOne`, dedicated `SKY_GLOW_PIPE`); D3D7 has no separate-alpha states, so alpha uses the same factors. No shader premultiplication. Regressions: `native_texture_interpolates_encoded_bytes_before_modulation` fixes UV at the midpoint of black/white texels and requires 128±1 rather than ~188; `native_effect_factors_match_display_system_states`, `framebuffer_clear_preserves_encoded_bytes`, `liquid_is_drawn_after_opaque_actors` cover blending. CPU mip construction and sampler selection are unchanged; exact legacy mip rounding is a separate unresolved prerequisite below. The older *Vertex lighting* narrative's references to sampled-texel re-encoding and linear-space texture filtering are superseded by this correction.

- **Self-illumination** (shader.wgsl): material uniform = `base_color` + `emissive` + glow flag (32 B; bind group key includes them). D3D7 order (RE in § meshes, *Vertex lighting* below): the vertex diffuse colour is `saturate(emissive + ambient + Σ diffuse lights)` — the emissive is **inside** the clamp (a fully emissive (1,1,1) surface shows the plain texture; the earlier post-clamp `tex * (light + emissive)` gave up to twice the texture) — and the stage output is `tex * tint * vertex colour`; for `glow_mask` submeshes stage 0 is `ADD` of texture alpha and the vertex colour, so `light = min(light + tex.a, 1)` (engine `saturate(a + lighting)`). All in the client's gamma space, see below.
- **Actor material overrides**: `ActorFrame::{emissive, specular, specular_power}` independently replace the corresponding `Submesh::{emissive, specular, shininess}` values for every body/rigid-part draw of that actor. `None` preserves the model material; RGB inputs use the same linear `c^2.2` storage as `Submesh`, recovered by `shader.wgsl::to_g` for D3D7 gamma-space vertex lighting. Power is an unconverted scalar. Overrides use dedicated actor instance attributes, not transform matrix slots; frame alpha is unchanged. Regression: `actor_material_overrides_preserve_base_and_emissive_gamma_space` compares absent and explicit base overrides and checks emissive output within 2/255.
- **Point lights** (`Scene::lights`, static): `LightGrid` built once in `upload`: dense 3D grid over the lights' AABB (+ range), cell edge 8 m, doubled until <= 2^20 cells; each light is written into every cell its sphere touches (sphere/box test); per cell the `CELL_LIGHTS = 16` best are kept, ranked by `(r+g+b) / (|cell centre - light|^2 + cell^2)`; dropped lights are simply absent in that cell (hard cap, no popping inside a cell, minor discontinuity at cell borders). GPU: three read-only storage buffers (lights as `vec4` pairs, cells `(first, count)`, index list, colours already in the client's gamma space) next to the globals uniform; the **vertex** stage looks up the cell of the vertex's world position and evaluates <= 8 of them with the D3D7 terms of *Vertex lighting* (`colour * N.L * 1/(a0 + a1 d + a2 d²) * spot`; the old half-lambert wrap and per-pixel evaluation are gone). Applies to every lit submesh (meshes, terrain, dungeon shells, characters). Cost is per vertex, independent of the light count. Sky has no lights; blended/additive submeshes use the same vertex stage, so blends are lit too.
- **Vertex lighting (D3D7 Gouraud)** (`shader.wgsl::vs` / `light_vertex` / `vertex_lights`; replaces the per-pixel evaluation). The client draws with `SHADEMODE = GOURAUD`, `LIGHTING = 1` and the fixed function pipeline, so lighting is evaluated **per vertex**, the result interpolated over the triangle and modulated with the texture (`COLOROP = MODULATE`); the fragment stage only samples, multiplies, adds specular and fogs. **The `_D3DMATERIAL7` of an archive mesh / CAT model (RE, randy31)**: `RViewPort_t::SetMaterial` @0x1004b199 (disassembled; it is the only material path of `RVisual_t::RenderWithTransparency` @0x1004d2d8 and of the CAT render `FUN_10056ed6`) compares and copies into the viewport's current `D3DMATERIAL7` slot (`this + slot·0x44 + 0xd8`) only `opac` → diffuse.a, `emis` → emissive rgb, `spec · shin_str` → specular rgb with specular.a = `shin_str`, and `shin` → power; **diffuse RGB and ambient RGB are never written**, they stay at the white `RViewPort_t::SetDefaultMaterial` @0x1004b61e stored (diffuse = ambient = (1,1,1,1), specular = `_DAT_10095900`, emissive 0). `RMaterial_t::InitD3DMaterial` @0x100409c6 (diffuse = `diff`, ambient = `ambi`, specular = `spec · shin_str`, emissive = `emis`, power = `shin`) is only called when the frame has material modifiers (`this+0xb0` ≠ 0: runtime effects, not data), by the billboard/sprite render `FUN_10013ab2` and by DisplaySystem effect objects (`FUN_10063dff`), never for scenery or characters. Consequence for the loaders: `base_color` = (1, 1, 1, `opac`) for every archive mesh (`mesh.rs`) and CAT material (`character.rs`) — the 653 untextured flat-colour submeshes of 1010001 are lit white like any other white material, `diff` and `ambi` (`ambi == diff` in 98 %) tint nothing — and `emissive` = `emis` as is (EMISSIVEMATERIALSOURCE keeps the device default MATERIAL; the luminance gate of `FUN_1004ac5f` only exists for `ConvertToLightmap`; 90 of the 3738 CAT materials carry a non-zero `emis`, e.g. tdroid/Swampy_tpage, so these models glow slightly as in the client). The material ambient colour is therefore constant white (no `Submesh` field), the ambient term is the device `AMBIENT`. Colour sources: render states 145/146/147 = 0 or the device defaults COLOR1/COLOR2/COLOR2 with FVF 0x112 (no vertex colour) both resolve to the material (D3D7 SDK/API evidence and the unchanged native VB format are detailed in the next item). Per vertex (world position `p`, unit normal `n`; `NORMALIZENORMALS` is 1 only for `s_eHardwareLevel & 2`, the port always normalises): `vertex colour = saturate(emissive + ambient + sun N.L + Σ colour · max(N.L, 0) · atten · spot)` (D3D clamps the summed vertex colour per channel; the light ambient terms are 0: `RLight_t::RLight_t` @0x1003fd4a sets ambient 0, diffuse = specular = colour), `atten = 1 / (a0 + a1 d + a2 d²)` for `d <= dvRange` (hard cut), spot cone `clamp((ρ − cos φ/2) / (cos θ/2 − cos φ/2), 0, 1)` (`dvFalloff` = 1), at most 8 lights from the grid cell of the vertex (sorted strongest first, LOD-disabled lights do not count). **The sun** is the directional `RLight_t` that `VisualAmbientLight_t::ConstructDirectionalLights` @DisplaySystem 0x10059e98 creates (white, group mask 1, direction (0,0,1) local) and `FUN_10059b98` @0x10059b98 programs from the `SunLight` tweak object (bound by `FUN_1005d589`: `GroundLightDirection`, `GroundLightCurrent`, `SpecularLightIntensity`, `AmbientLightCurrent`) whenever a visual type draws with another light and afterwards puts the ground light back (`FUN_1003f3db`, `FUN_10054e07`, `FUN_1005c334`, `FUN_10067f40` load the mono water light `DAT_10150264` / the ground light `DAT_10150268` this way), so the ground light is the default state: `D3DLIGHT7.diffuse = intensity · GroundLightCurrent` (intensity 1.0), `specular = intensity · SpecularLightIntensity · GroundLightCurrent`, direction `GroundLightDirection`, ambient 0. `SpecularLightIntensity` is `Float … : 1.0` in the Rubi-Ka / Shadowlands / Outerspace sun tweaks and 0.01 in `Tweak_Playfield_Alien_LandingCraft` (`Sky::sun_specular` → `Environment::sun_specular`). The device `AMBIENT` is the per-channel maximum of the `AmbientLightCurrent` / record colours (`VisualAmbientLight_t::AddAmbientLight`, `FUN_10059d82` → `Randy_t::SetAmbientLight`), and the sun counts as one of the 8 hardware lights (whether `RVisual_t::CullLights` always keeps it is UNRESOLVED; the grid does not reserve a slot for it). **Specular**: `RMaterial_t::UpdateSpecular` @0x10040ff8 (called by the CAT/`RMaterial_t(name, tex, diff, spec, ambi, emis, shin, str, opac)` ctor @0x10041043, disassembled) sets `D3DRS_SPECULARENABLE` (0x1d) = `spec != black && shin_str > 0 && shin >= 0`; the **archive reader** (`FUN_1004132d`, `RMaterial_t(ObjectArchive_c*)`) does *not* call it, it keeps the stored delta state (rs 29 as authored; it does not call `SetOpacity` either), so `mesh.rs` rightly uses the stored render state 29 (8174 of the SimpleMesh submeshes, 403 of 1010026) and `character.rs::submesh_for` the `UpdateSpecular` rule (1027 of 3738 CAT materials; none has power 0). `ao_scene::Submesh::{specular, shininess}` carry `spec · shin_str` / `shin` (zero ⇒ off). Lighting adds `Cs · Σ Ls · atten · spot · (N·H)^power` for lights with `N.L > 0` and `power > 0`, `H = |L + V|` with `V` to the eye (`LOCALVIEWER` = 1, device reset `FUN_10041ede`), the point/spot specular colour being its diffuse colour (`docs/screens.md` §2) and the sun's `intensity · SpecularLightIntensity · colour`; the sum is saturated and **added after the texture stage** (`saturate(tex * tint * vertex colour + specular)`, then fog). **Colour pipeline (gamma space)**: the client computes everything — lighting sum, MODULATE stage, specular add, table fog — on 8 bit framebuffer values (textures are not decoded). The scene contract carries colours as `c^2.2` and the target is UNORM, so the shader recovers the client's values (`c^(1/2.2)`, exact inverse of the producers' power curve; lights and ambient/sun/fog are converted once on the CPU, material colours per vertex), samples encoded UNORM texels directly, evaluates the whole surface (including fog, mix in gamma space) in gamma space and writes the gamma-space result directly to UNORM (`shader.wgsl::shade`, `shade_env`), storing the same 8 bit values as the client. This replaces the earlier linear-space sum (e.g. ambient 0.5 + sun 0.5 saturates to 1.0 in D3D and gave 0.69 before; test `lighting_sum_is_formed_in_gamma_space`). `Submesh::prelit` surfaces (dungeon room shells, shadow-mapped ground) carry the additive light in the vertex colour in the same D3D space (dungeon bytes `v/255` as is, terrain `palette + sun_g · N.L − 0.8 · ambient_g`) and evaluate `saturate(vertex + 0.8 · ambient + point/spot diffuse)`; no sun, no specular. Remaining differences: ordinary-resource mip generation retains its earlier approximation; the sun/ambient defaults of scenes without sky tweaks (`1 − ambient` sun) are still the old guess; per-vertex light ranking by grid cell instead of `CullLights`. Differences from the earlier per-pixel path: no more normal flip toward the eye (D3D has no two-sided lighting; unlit backs are as dark as in the client), large flat quads show interpolated corner lighting. Fog stays per pixel (`FOGTABLEMODE` LINEAR table fog, `FUN_10041ede`). Sky has its own `vs_sky`/`shade_sky` (unlit). Measured in the windowed viewer (`AOMAC_PERF=1 AOMAC_NOVSYNC=1 aomac view pf <id> --mute`, default start view): pf 505 2.1 → 2.4 ms/frame, pf 566 2.1 → 2.2 ms/frame (gamma pipeline, sun specular). Tests: `ao-render` `diffuse_light_is_evaluated_per_vertex_and_interpolated`, `specular_term_is_added_after_the_texture_stage`, `lighting_sum_is_formed_in_gamma_space`, `emissive_is_saturated_with_the_lighting`, `sun_specular_follows_the_light_intensity`; `mesh::tests::diffuse_is_white_opacity_is_kept_and_default_is_opaque_culled`.
- **Missing vertex-colour fallback — API-proven PASS (D3D7)**: the port's existing material fallback is correct; no lighting maths changes. Native reset `10041ede` (`10042118..10042140`) sets COLORVERTEX=1, 145=COLOR1, 146/147=COLOR2; `ConvertToLightmap1004ac5f` explicitly sets 145..147=MATERIAL. The ordinary archive `SimpleMesh` lighting input really has no diffuse/specular component: deserializer `10048a5c` copies the 16-byte `vb_desc`, passes its FVF at `+8` unchanged to `100488ed`, and copies the raw vertex blob with `memcpy`. `100488ed` → `VertexBuffer_c100114b1` → `10011797` stores that FVF, computes stride/count from it and the byte length, and `10011745` rebuilds a `D3DVERTEXBUFFERDESC` with the same FVF (allocation flags are reconstructed, not blindly copied). `CreateVertexBuffer10024a77` forwards the descriptor to `IDirect3D7` vtable+0x14. If a hardware buffer is rebuilt, `1004872b` → copy constructor `1001153a/10011853` copies format, stride, count and raw bytes without adding colours. `SimpleMesh1004f154` selects that stored/rebuilt VB; `1001fdba` → `RenderTriangleList10021a88` dereferences the wrapper/implementation to its actual `IDirect3DVertexBuffer7*`, then `10021971` calls device+0x80 (`DrawIndexedPrimitiveVB`, triangle list4, flags0). There is no alternate FVF draw argument and no inserted colour channel in this path. **Primary API evidence:** the original DX7 SDK (built 2 September 1999) documents [COLORVERTEX and COLOR1/COLOR2 defaults](https://library.thedatadungeon.com/directx7/devdoc/live/directx/vbimref_26aq.content.htm) and vertex diffuse/specular colours only “[if specified](https://library.thedatadungeon.com/directx7/devdoc/live/directx/imover_7pyc.content.htm)”. Its Triangle tutorial creates [D3DVERTEX position/normal/UV data](https://library.thedatadungeon.com/directx7/devdoc/live/directx/imtut_7350.content.htm), sets only [material ambient=(1,1,0) and white device AMBIENT, documented as yellow](https://library.thedatadungeon.com/directx7/devdoc/live/directx/imtut_3r1g.content.htm), then [draws D3DFVF_VERTEX](https://library.thedatadungeon.com/directx7/devdoc/live/directx/imtut_91yc.content.htm) without changing the default AMBIENTMATERIALSOURCE=COLOR2. Missing vertex colours therefore leave material-only lighting, rather than supplying a fabricated white/zero colour. **Secondary corroboration, not implementation:** Wine's [D3D7 conformance tests](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wine-mirror/wine/wine-5.0/dlls/ddraw/tests/ddraw7.c) (`test_fog_process_vertices`) expect material specular `(0,1,0,.5)` to produce `0x8000ff00` with position/normal-only vertices; [test_specular_lighting](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wine-mirror/wine/master/dlls/ddraw/tests/ddraw7.c) checks lit diffuse zero separately from the unlit white default. [Wine's Windows test results](https://test.winehq.org/data/tests/ddraw:ddraw7.html) corroborate the API contract, not a runtime exercised by this project. No Wine code was copied and no D3D7 runtime, build, test, screenshot or login was run for this documentation-only closure. This does not close the separately scoped backdrop/ConvertToLightmap emissive-colour inference in `docs/screens.md`.
- **Sky** (`Scene::sky`, separate from world instances): pre-rendered with authored blend/culling, no depth writes, depth Always, native shared camera projection/clipping and vertex eye-depth source fog. Normalized geometry scales by far/1000 then receives camera translation and authored/per-frame rotation; SingleClouds already supply metre-scaled geometry. `sky_vertex` saturates additive lighting/emission before texture MODULATE/gain, never `rgb*(1+emissive)`. Generic resources apply the native first-material reset below; raw archive emission is not their final material.
- Culling: per-mesh bounding sphere (AABB centre, max vertex distance) scaled by the largest transform axis length, tested against the 6 view-frustum planes on the CPU each frame.
- wgpu 29 (not 24): `block v0.1.6` (future-incompat warning) is pulled in by `metal` 0.31-0.33, i.e. by wgpu-hal 24-28; wgpu-hal 29 uses objc2-metal and the warning is gone.
- **Single world pass / transient targets**: `Renderer::render` keeps sky, actor priorities 0–2 interleaved with terrain base/shadow stages, opaque world + actor list3, liquids + actor list4, blended world and actor lists5/6/10/7 in their existing order within one render pass. Material pipelines/blend/depth state are unchanged. Native ordered depth clears (DisplaySystem `1000bc0b`, Randy `1004b75a`, including mesh-effect3) are full-viewport draws with depth Always/write1 and color writes disabled, not pass boundaries. wgpu29 `TextureUsages::TRANSIENT` is used for MSAA color and depth (a no-op on unsupported backends); both discard stores, with MSAA resolved once at world-pass end. Single-sample output remains stored. Viewport sprites still run after resolve to preserve their original per-pixel blending. Added, not run by this worker: `cargo test --release -p ao-render ordered_depth_clear_matches_split_pass_pixels` compares all RGBA bytes against split-pass attachment clears at every adapter-supported sample count, including rejected geometry, two ordered clears, alpha blending and a diagonal multisampled edge; interior pixels also have explicit expected colors.
- **Enhanced MSAA capabilities**: requesting adapter-specific sample counts requires enabling wgpu29 `TEXTURE_ADAPTER_SPECIFIC_FORMAT_FEATURES` when the adapter offers it, not merely reading `get_texture_format_features`. Without that device feature, use the guaranteed format capabilities instead. Supported MSAA levels are the intersection for output color and `Depth32Float`; requests clamp downward to that intersection. This fixes validation for adapter-only 2×/8× levels without changing the retail-faithful default of 1 sample (MSAA off).
- Camera: `Scene::spawn` (looking at `spawn_look_at`, else horizontally toward the bounds centre) if set, else a 3/4 view framing the instance bounds. `--eye/--at` override it for both the screenshot and the interactive viewer.
- Viewer controls: WASD move, Space/E up, Ctrl/Q down, Shift ×5 speed, scroll = speed ×1.15 per notch, hold right mouse = mouse-look (cursor locked), Esc quits. Title shows FPS, position, speed. `AOMAC_NOVSYNC=1` disables vsync and keeps rendering uncapped. On macOS, vsynced rendering yields the AppKit runloop using `WaitUntil` at the current monitor's native refresh period instead of continuously filling the drawable queue; unavailable native refresh information retains GPU vsync without inventing a rate. Backing-scale changes update both GUI drawing and cursor conversion. `AOMAC_PERF=1` prints start camera, scene bounds and per-0.5-second frame/render CPU timings, surface acquisition, GUI/frame work, reconfigure, present, worst frame-entry gap, attempts/presented frames, visible instances and draw calls.
- Headless: `ao_render::render_to_png(scene, eye, look_at, w, h, path)`.
- CLI (`--client DIR` default `~/Games/ProjectRubiKa/client`):
  - `aomac install [--client DIR]` → `ao_install::run`
  - `aomac view mesh <id>` / `view char <id> [--anim A --time S]` / `view pf <id>` / `view pf --list` / `view demo [--count N]`
  - on any view: `--screenshot out.png [--eye x,y,z] [--at x,y,z] [--size WxH]` renders offscreen and exits (defaults: `ao_render::default_view`, 1280x800).
- Perf (M4 Pro, release, 1280x800 logical = 2560x1600 px window, 4x MSAA, `AOMAC_NOVSYNC=1`, ms/frame; before = old renderer that drew every instance, no culling, treated all non-opaque blends as cutouts): pf 505 spawn view 2.95 -> 2.1-2.5; pf 566 spawn view 1.8 -> 0.85 (wgpu 29) ; pf 505 from 2.5 km up with ~26.6k statels visible 2.3 -> 5.3 (6689 draws; 5.7k of them are per-instance sorted AlphaBlend submeshes, CPU 2.7 ms; with those batched as opaque it is 4.2, so true sorted blending costs ~1 ms per 5.7k instances: producers should prefer AlphaTest for foliage cutouts); pf 566 from 500 m up 1.7 -> 1.9. Spawn-view numbers include scene changes by other slices between measurements. Demo `--count 10000`: 1.5-2.2 ms.

### Shared actor resources

Performance audit items 11–12: `ao-render/src/actors.rs` interns immutable archive texture uploads across actor
model keys. Identity includes the native `TextureKey`, decoded source format, dimensions, texture quality,
compression, better-mip policy and a stdlib content fingerprint of the decoded bytes. `RecordStore` remaps
canonical type 1010004 to quality families 1010016/1010017 independently of the upload policy; the fingerprint
prevents those decoded variants aliasing even at equal dimensions. It is a non-cryptographic fingerprint for
trusted immutable RDB assets, not a collision-proof content-addressed store. Only native bitmap families 1010004/1010006/1010009/1010011/1010021/1010022
qualify; generated-source textures, type-0 keys and synthetic composite namespaces never share. In particular,
CAT skin composites use `0x40000000 | id` (`ao-formats/src/character/actor.rs` and `player.rs`) and must remain
model-local even when their keys collide.

Models own reference-counted texture resources and clone wgpu views into their material bindings. The lookup
holds weak references only; replacement/removal prunes dead entries, and clearing a zone empties it. A policy
or source change selects a different upload, including when supplied through `update_actor_model`. No pixel
data is copied into a permanent GPU cache. `actor_texture_resources()` reports unique model-retained textures
and estimated encoded mip bytes, not total Metal VRAM; `actor_texture_uploads()` counts actual actor uploads.

`Host::actor_models` and `actor_model_updates` carry `Arc<Scene>` so uploading an existing model does not
deep-copy its decoded textures or mesh arrays. Actors using the same model key already share its material,
index and rigid-vertex buffers. Different model keys keep separate mutable geometry buffers because
`update_actor_model` rewrites them in place; texture sharing must not alias these writes.

Regression coverage: `immutable_actor_textures_share_uploads_pixels_and_reclaim` compares offscreen pixels
for two models, counts one shared texture upload (white placeholders remain model-local), proves generated
textures stay independent, checks policy/source variants, and verifies last-owner reclamation and independent
geometry writes. `actor_texture_identity_excludes_generated_namespaces_and_tracks_upload_policy` covers
synthetic namespaces and every policy field. These are runnable checks, not benchmark results.
`actor_model_handoff_keeps_the_original_scene_allocation` proves both Host queues retain the original allocation
and release their references on scene replacement. Offscreen actor image helpers borrow either owned or shared
scenes, so capture/test callers can consume the same handoff without cloning scene contents.

### Native window move profiling (2026-10-07)

- Before pacing, a real muted `aomac play` login window (PID-owned, exclusive live lock, unlocked screen) was moved by focused HID mouse events over 120×60 logical points and live-resized by 120×80. No account login was needed. Normal login rendering was approximately 4.2–4.7 ms/frame, with 3.5–4.0 ms/frame spent acquiring the surface drawable. One instrumented move-start aggregate reached 8.54 ms/frame, acquisition 7.79 ms, and worst frame-entry gap 169.34 ms; a separate baseline reached 21.56 ms/frame. These are half-second aggregates, not per-frame percentiles.
- `sample <owned-pid> 6 1` during the move attributed 3,820 main-thread samples to `CAMetalLayer::nextDrawable` (3,777 in its semaphore wait), while AppKit move events use that same thread. During live resize, reconfigure averaged about 0.03–0.12 ms/frame, versus roughly 4 ms acquisition; target allocation was not the dominant observed cause in this login scene. This evidence motivates yielding before acquisition while preserving display synchronization, rather than disabling vsync or suppressing rendering while moving.
- Scheduling follows winit 0.30.13 `macos/monitor.rs` native CGDisplayMode/CVDisplayLink refresh reporting and `macos/app_state.rs` native `WaitUntil` waker. Late frames establish a new deadline instead of catch-up bursts. Regression: `native_pacing_uses_refresh_and_does_not_catch_up_missed_frames`; backing-scale regression: `gui_scale_rounds_and_keeps_at_least_one_pixel`.
- **Post-fix real-window acceptance: not done (user using Mac).** The user prohibited further windows/input/screenshots before post-fix profiling could run. No before/after speedup or real-window PASS is claimed; native acquisition under GPU contention can still block. Headless scheduling regressions do not measure AppKit move smoothness.

## meshes

Implementation: `crates/ao-formats/src/{archive.rs,mesh.rs}`; survey tool `cargo run --release -p ao-formats --example mesh_stats`.
Ground truth: Ghidra 12.1.4 decompilation of `randy31.dll` (the "Randy" engine; PE image base 0x10000000, addresses below are VAs): `RRefFrame_t::Archive` @10044da8, `RRefFrame_t::RRefFrame_t(ObjectArchive_c*)` @10045a2f, `RRefFrame_t::UpdateWorldMatrix` @10044fa2, `RVisualData_t::Archive` @1004ea04 (the `FAFTriMeshData_t` base), vertex-buffer (de)serialisers @1004883e / @10048a5c, `RDeltaState::Archive` @1002f145. No community documentation of this format was found; everything here comes from the DLL plus the data.

### Record types
- `rdb_1010001` (9925 records, 352 MB): "static meshes" — scenery, buildings, props, trees, doors, lamps, *and* animated/effect meshes. Ids 1414…289317. A record is a whole node tree (one model), not a single triangle soup.
- `rdb_1010026` (699 records, 14.6 MB): same container and classes, **every id also exists in 1010001**, ~3× fewer triangles (e.g. 1495: 176 → 12 tris, 134 → 18 verts), single node, keeps the same textures: a **reduced-detail mesh**. Code evidence (type ids 1010001 = 0xf6951, 1010026 = 0xf696a): `n3VisualDynel_t::HasMesh` (N3.dll @10019514) accepts both as "plain mesh" (the CAT pair 1010002/1010027 = 0xf6952/0xf696b is `HasCatMesh` @100194f9); `RDBMesh_t::ReduceMesh` (DisplaySystem.dll @10077095) derives a 0xf696a `DbObject` with the same id from a 0xf6951 one by keeping only the four `SimpleMesh`es with the most vertices (returns null when the source has < 4) and cloning the rest of the node; the one consumer found is `AOPlayfieldCityHolderClient_c::EnableVisibility` (Gamecode.dll @1012e58b), which builds `VisualMesh_t`s from `Identity{0xf696a, id}` for the `HouseVisualData` list of a player-city holder and registers them with `LODManager::AddMesh` (distant city houses). So it is a LOD proxy used by the city/LOD path; statels and ordinary visuals reference 1010001. `[INFERENCE: the shipped records do not all have ≤ 4 submeshes (num_meshes 1…21), so they were generated by a different/earlier tool than ReduceMesh; the distance switch inside LODManager is in a DLL that was not decompiled]`. `mesh::decode_record_into(.., MESH_LOW_TYPE, ..)` decodes it; `decode_mesh_into`/`load_mesh` use 1010001.
- Textures referenced by meshes: `rdb_1010004` (32 629 refs: JPEG or PNG-with-alpha) and `rdb_1010011` (5 refs); decoded by `texture::load_texture`.
- `1010002`/`1010003`/`1010027` are character models/animations, see § characters. `1010013` (363 small records beginning `02 00 00 00`, floats follow) and `1010025` (one record of animal names "Beetle/Bird/Bunny") are not meshes of this format.

### Container: Funcom `ObjectArchive` (little endian)
```
u32 version = 3
u32 range_count
range_count x { i32 time_ms; char name[32] } named animation segment starts ("idle","walk",…), 0xCD padded; 36 bytes each
u32 w0, u32 w1(=1)                           not used by the reader (w0 is 0 or a small packed value such as 0x07000700)
u32 name_count (<=256)
name_count x { cstr kind; cstr name }        kind = "" starts a class (name = class), else a member "digit"; names are indexed globally 0..name_count
u32 object_count
(object_count + 1) x object                  object 0 is a holder with a single member "obj" (ref) = index of the root object
object := u32 type_id, u32 version(=1), u32 member_count, member_count x member
member := u8 name_index, u32 type, u32 elem_size, u32 total_size, bytes[total_size]
trailing: 12+ zero bytes
```
Evidence: record 1010001/287726 (311 B, an empty `RRefFrame_t`): table of 9 names (`RRefFrame_t`, `__class_id__`, `grp_mask`, `local_pos`, `local_rot`, `scale`, `conn`, `chld_cnt`, `obj`), holder member `obj`=0, object 0 has 7 members, file ends exactly 12 bytes after the last member. Records with `range_count` > 0: 7798 (3 ranges), 36077 (idle, walk), 75392 …; before handling the ranges 16 records failed (grouped as "implausible name table"/"no root"), after: all parse.

The range header is retained as `Archive::animation_ranges`: DisplaySystem `10076adc` reads version 3 through `1006ab17`, which expands each signed millisecond timestamp/name into a 40-byte native event (`name[32]`, zero word, `f32 time_ms` at +36). Async callback `10071203` passes resource +0x34 to `1006d7b9`, copying those events into `VisualMesh_t+0xa4`; SpeciesBody `1011815f` converts the float milliseconds with ×0.001 and uses the next event/tree total as the segment end. Wildlife models reuse `load_animated_mesh`/`NodeRig` rigid frame, UV and visibility poses, not static substitute geometry. `GetMeshHeight` (`1006bcfb`) is twice the largest geometry-frame **bounding sphere radius**, not vertex Y extent (`BVolume_t::Archive`, Randy `10029f2d`, radius +0x14).

The live `Wildlife` Login preference is loaded from the existing persisted `Prefs.xml` at startup and observed from HUD dvalues every frame (including the existing `ToggleAllEffects` cascade). Disabling destroys `LifeWorld` species and submitted life instances; enabling prepares native district requests again. Background life results are playfield-id guarded, and world changes drop the old life manager. Runtime actor identities use the manager's stable ids, not indices shifted by expiry. The installed-data regression `installed_wildlife_meshes_animate_and_disable_destroys_instances` covers real catalog mesh poses and removal; `wildlife_sensors_use_native_characters_and_dead_flags` uses captured Type50000 updates. These checks were added but not run by this worker.

Wildlife danger spheres are **not character bounds**: GC `100b85dd` appends the explicit `(position,radius)` list, cleared by `100b85a3` at the end of `Life::Run`. FX9's producer (`100af6f4`) uses radius50 at its FX position; DisplaySystem lightning activation callbacks `100508bd`/`10050d3e` create those FX9 events at actual authored bolt source points. AFCM module9 message0x119 (`100b29f1`, registered `100b68ad`) is a four-float danger receiver: SandyInterface `PlaySample` (`10002d98`, send `1000347d`) emits the positional source and sample +0x280 scare radius after its playback gates. Fly landing accepts dead (`+0x138` flag0x10) **Type50000 characters**, not Type51050 corpse props.

Member `type` codes seen (self-describing; matches `fun::Message_c::Add*` in the DLL): `0` bool/u8, `3` int32 (arrays when size > 4), `6` string (`u32 len` + bytes, no NUL), `9` raw blob (`u32 len`-prefixed for `vertices`/`triangles`, bare 16 bytes for `vb_desc`), `0xa` f32, `0xc` Vector3, `0xd` Quaternion (x,y,z,w), `0xf` 4x4 matrix (16 f32), `0x10` RGB f32 colour, `0x11` object reference(s) (i32 each; `-1` = null; one member holds all refs of an array, e.g. `mesh`, `chld`, `tch_text`). Repeated scalar members (`rst_type`, `rst_value`, `tch_type`, `tch_text`) appear once per element and must be read in order. `__class_id__` is a per-file running number, not a class tag; objects are classified by their member names. The 1-byte `name_index` limits a record to 256 names.

### Object classes (identified by members)
| object | members used | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| frame node (`RRefFrame_t`, `RTriMesh_t`, `FAFPointLight_t`, `FAFCollisionSphere_c`, `FAFAttractor_t`, `RRefFrameConnector`, …) | `local_pos` v3, `local_rot` quat, `scale` f32 (always 1.0 in the data), `anim_matrix` m4 (optional), `chld`/`chld_cnt`, `conn`, `anim`, `grp_mask` (always -1 on meshes) | transform tree. Roots may be `RTriMesh_t` itself (class list then lacks `RRefFrame_t`) |
| mesh node (`RTriMesh_t`) | node members + `data` ref, `delta_state` ref, `prio` (3/5/6 draw order), `enable_light`, `is_cloned` | |
| mesh data (`FAFTriMeshData_t`) | `name`, `anim_pos`, `anim_rot`, `num_meshes`, `mesh` refs, `bvol` | holds `num_meshes` (1…57) `SimpleMesh` refs |
| `SimpleMesh` | `material` ref, `trilist` ref, `vb_desc`, `vertices` | one draw call |
| `FAFMaterial_t` | `delta_state` ref, `env_texture` ref, `diff/spec/ambi/emis` RGB, `shin`, `shin_str`, `opac` | `RMaterial_t::Archive` @10040916 (writer; reader `FUN_1004132d`; offsets +0x2c diff, +0x38 spec, +0x44 ambi, +0x50 emis, +0x5c env, +0x60 shin, +0x64 shin_str, +0x68 opac, +0x70 delta_state). `RMaterial_t::InitD3DMaterial` @100409c6 fills a `_D3DMATERIAL7` (diffuse = (`diff`, alpha = `opac`), specular = `spec`×`shin_str`, ambient, emissive, power = `shin`) but the scenery/CAT render path (`RViewPort_t::SetMaterial` @1004b199) only copies `opac`, `emis`, `spec`×`shin_str`, `shin`: **opacity, emissive, specular and power are used, `diff` / `ambi` are not** (see *Vertex lighting*) |
| `RDeltaState` | `rst_count`,`rst_type`,`rst_value` pairs (D3D7 `D3DRENDERSTATE_*`), `tstv_count`,`tstm_count`,`tst_type`,`tst_value`, `tch_count`, `tch_type`+`tch_text` per channel | render-state delta; applied by `RVisual_t::ApplyStateChanges` @1004cbe1 (node, before the mesh loop) and `FUN_10040aa0` ← `RViewPort_t::SetMaterial` @1004b199 (material, per `SimpleMesh`, later ⇒ wins) |
| `FAFTexture_t` → `AnarchyTexCreator_t` | `creator` ref → `type` (rdb type, 1010004) + `inst` (rdb id) | texture record |
| `TriList` | `triangles` = `u32 byte_len` + u16 triples | triangle list, 16-bit indices |
| `BVolume_t` | `sph_pos`, `sph_radius`, `min_pos`, `max_pos` | bounds, ignored |
| `FAFAnim_t` | `name`, `tot_time`, `loop`, `rot_keys` (`u32` byte length, then `{x, y, z, w, time}` ×20 bytes), `trans_keys` (`{x, y, z, time}` ×16), `vis_keys` (8), `uv_keys` | node keyframes: the static decode bakes the `anim_matrix` (= the pose at time 0) and ignores them; `mesh::NodeRig` evaluates them (doors, animated items: docs/zone/doors.md §4) |

`vb_desc` = `{u32 16, u32 0x10000, u32 FVF, u32 vertex_count}`; FVF is **0x112** (D3DFVF_XYZ | NORMAL | TEX1) in all 50 159 SimpleMeshes → 32-byte vertices `pos[3] normal[3] uv[2]` (f32). `vertices` = `u32 byte_len` + `vertex_count * 32` bytes. The loader also understands diffuse/specular/extra UV sets generally from the FVF bits.
Native upload preserves this colour-free format rather than inventing colour bytes: `10048a5c` → `100488ed` copies FVF and raw bytes; `10011797/10011745` derives stride/count and creates the VB with that FVF through `CreateVertexBuffer10024a77` (D3D7+0x14). A rebuilt VB uses `1004872b` → `10011853`, retaining format/stride/count and memcpy bytes. `SimpleMesh1004f154` → `1001fdba` → `10021a88/10021971` submits the actual VB through D3D7+0x80, with no replacement FVF parameter. See the API-proven missing-colour fallback above.

Render states observed (`rst_type`/`rst_value`; the engine is **D3D7**: `_D3DMATERIAL7`, `_D3DRENDERSTATETYPE`, ids below are D3D7's) over all of 1010001: 15 ALPHATESTENABLE=1 (1363 deltas), 27 ALPHABLENDENABLE=1 (1639) / =0 (1591), 22 CULLMODE=1 (D3DCULL_NONE, 1708), 29 SPECULARENABLE=1 (8395), 145 DIFFUSEMATERIALSOURCE=0 (519). **Never present: 19/20 SRC/DESTBLEND, 24/25 ALPHAREF/FUNC, 14 ZWRITEENABLE, any CULLMODE other than 1.** (145–148 are D3D7's `DIFFUSE/SPECULAR/AMBIENT/EMISSIVE_MATERIALSOURCE`; 0 = take it from the material instead of the vertex colour; `FUN_1004ac5f` @1004ac5f sets 145..147=0 and 148 = (emissive ≤ threshold) on every material, `RenderWithTransparency` sets 146/148=1 when a per-instance colour is bound.) Foliage keeps its states on the **node's** `delta_state` ("alpha", node 11 of 1010001/6273 → `rst 15=1`) while the texture is on the material's `delta_state`; the loader merges both, **material wins** per state id. `tch_type` is the channel (0 = diffuse). Texture-stage deltas only occur as `tst (1, 7)` (COLOROP=ADD, 1011×) and `tst (2, 34)` (COLORARG1=TEXTURE|ALPHAREPLICATE, 1953×).

**Where the states come from (`FUN_10040645` @10040645 = RMaterial transparency preset, mode chosen from a `[n]` prefix of the material name or automatically from the texture's alpha channel, `FUN_1004761a`):** mode 0 texture alpha = transparency (`0x74`/`0xbd` flags, 27=1); 1 alpha test (27=0, 15=1, ALPHAFUNC=GREATER, ALPHAREF=0x80); 2 alpha test ref 0x1e **and** blend (27=1,15=1), z-write off; 3 blend only, z-write off; 4 additive (27=1, SRC=ONE, DEST=ONE, TSS colour op = SELECTARG1(texture)), z-write off; 5 (automatic for textures with alpha when ≥ 2 texture stages and no transparency flag): stage 0 = `texture.alpha(replicated) + diffuse` (COLOROP ADD), stage 1 = `texture.rgb × stage0` (COLOROP MODULATE) with the same texture bound twice, 27=0 — i.e. **the texture alpha channel is a self-illumination mask** (this is the `(27,0)` + `tst (1,7)` pair in the data, ≈1000 materials). `RMaterial_t::IsTwoSided` @10040aff = rst 22 == 1 (NONE); `RMaterial_t::SetOpacity` @10040f61 sets the "transparent" flag `0xbd` (render list 6, back-to-front sort) when `opac` < threshold or the texture has alpha, but if a delta state exists it is overwritten by rst 27==1. `RTriMesh_t::Render` @10049761 puts the mesh in render list 6 when any material is transparent or frame transparency < 1, else list 3 (the `prio` member of the node is 3/5/6: opaque / ? / transparent, i.e. the same sort-list choice; the renderer sorts blended draws itself, `prio` is not exported). Randy never sets SRC/DESTBLEND for mesh materials (only for the sprite/glow classes: `RSprite::EnableAdditiveRendering` @10013128, DisplaySystem @859: SRC=SRCALPHA(5), DEST=ONE(2), z-write off), so plain blend uses the D3D default pair `[INFERENCE: SRCALPHA/INVSRCALPHA, the only pair that makes 27=1 visible]`. Randy never changes CULLMODE globally, so the device default (D3DCULL_CCW; front = clockwise in D3D's left-handed space) applies to every material without `rst 22=1`.

### Transform rule (from `UpdateWorldMatrix` @10044fa2)
Row-vector D3D convention (`v' = v · M`, translation in row 3): `local = R(local_rot)ᵀ · scale, translation = local_pos`; if `anim_matrix` exists: `local = anim_matrix · local`; `world = local · parent_world`. Quaternion → matrix is the standard one, transposed (decompiled @1006e393). `anim_matrix` of mesh nodes equals the object's 3ds-Max node transform (= `FAFTriMeshData_t.anim_pos/anim_rot`, e.g. `Rx(+90°)` for Max Z-up → Y-up: 1010001/214381, `anim_pos` (-0.539, 0.452, -0.454) = `anim_matrix` row 3) and is what makes the vertices upright; `anim_pos/anim_rot` themselves are **not** applied again. Vertices are stored in the mesh node's object space.

### Coordinate conversion (loader output)
Source space is **left-handed, Y up, 1 unit = 1 m** (D3D9; triangles are clockwise-front: for record 214381 the numeric cross product of tri (0,1,2) equals the stored vertex normal −Z). The loader bakes the node tree into one `Mesh`, then **negates Z** of positions and normals (→ right-handed, +Y up) and **reverses triangle winding** (flipped back when a node matrix has negative determinant). UVs are untouched (D3D origin top-left == `Texture` row order). Verified: over all 7.4 M triangles of 1010001 the CCW geometric normal agrees with the vertex normals for 97.7 % (7 217 373 vs 172 241, the rest are flat/double-sided strips); barrel texture 6153 (1010004) renders un-mirrored. **Anything positioned in AO world space (statel placements!) must undergo the same Z negation** (`p' = (x, y, -z)`, rotations conjugated accordingly) to line up with the converted meshes.

Submeshes are merged per `(texture, blend, two_sided, base_color)` (the loader's `MatKey`). Mapping onto `ao-scene::Submesh`:
- `blend`: rst 15=1 → `AlphaTest` (also when 27=1, see mode 2: the cutout survives without depth sorting); else rst 27=1 → `Additive` if DESTBLEND=ONE and SRCBLEND ∈ {ONE, SRCALPHA}, else `AlphaBlend`; else `Opaque`. Untextured submeshes can be blended/tested too (alpha = `opac`).
- `two_sided` = rst 22 == NONE; **default is culled** (back faces of the CCW-front scene-space triangles).
- `base_color` = (1, 1, 1, `opac`). The D3D material diffuse / ambient RGB of a mesh draw is white (`SetDefaultMaterial` @1004b61e; `RViewPort_t::SetMaterial` never copies `diff` / `ambi`, see *Vertex lighting* in § renderer), so the material colours of the data tint nothing (in 1010001 only 653 untextured submeshes have a non-white `diff`, 0 textured ones; `ambi == diff` in 98 % of materials). The earlier loader used `diff` as the tint of flat-colour materials (hull parts green/black): not what the client draws.
- `emissive` (contract colour `c^2.2` like every colour) = `emis` as is: EMISSIVEMATERIALSOURCE (rs 148) stays at the device default MATERIAL and `SetMaterial` copies `emis` into the D3D emissive. `FUN_1004ac5f` @1004ac5f (rs 145/146/147 = 0, 148 = `lum <= 0` with `lum = 0.299R+0.587G+0.114B`, `FUN_1004a8c0`, threshold `_DAT_1009fda8` = 0.0f) has only two callers, `RTriMesh_t::ConvertToLightmap` @0x1004980f directly and via `FUN_1004ad93` (meshes converted to lightmaps), so ordinary meshes keep their own node states (the login backdrop nodes carry rs 145 = 146 = 147 = 0, 148 = 1 and the engine reset leaves 145/146/147/148 = COLOR1/COLOR2/COLOR2/MATERIAL, docs/screens.md §2); the former luminance gate was a no-op for data ≥ 0 (no negative `emis` exists). D3D adds the emissive to the lighting sum **inside** the clamp, then multiplies the texture; values in the data are almost always (1,1,1) (fully self-lit: lamps, signs, windows) or small greys. A per-instance emissive override exists (`RRefFrame_t::SetEmissive` @10045329 → frame +0x8c → `RVisual_t::RenderWithTransparency` @1004d2d8 sets rs 148=1 and passes it in the `_D3DMATERIAL7`); it is runtime state (effects), not in the data, so not emitted.
  **GenericMesh exception (fresh trace 2026-10-15):** DisplaySystem `1005e716` calls resource loader `10061505`, which traverses every loaded mesh-data node, takes its **first SimpleMesh's material resource**, and writes diffuse(+0x2c)=white, emissive(+0x50)=black, ambient(+0x44)=black, specular(+0x38)=black, then calls `UpdateSpecular10040ff8`. It does this **before** MeshIndex selection, mutating shared material identity, not every material slot indiscriminately. `decode_generic_mesh` now gathers these first-material identities from all nodes and clears emission/specular for every reference to those identities; unrelated materials and ordinary raw/static mesh decoding retain authored emission. D3D viewport ambient/diffuse remain white (`1004b61e`/`1004b199`), so the generic resource's black author ambient does not disable inherited device ambient. Regressions: `generic_resource_resets_raw_cloud_material_emission_and_specular`, `generic_material_reset_follows_shared_identity_not_submesh_slot`.
- `glow_mask` = opaque, textured, and texture stage 0 has COLOROP = ADD (`tst (1,7)`) with COLORARG1 containing D3DTA_ALPHAREPLICATE 0x20 (`tst (2,34)`) — exactly the stage setup of mode 5 of `FUN_10040645` (stage 0 = `tex.a + diffuse(lighting)`, stage 1 = `tex.rgb * stage0`, ALPHAOP disabled, 27=0). Stage layout in the archive: `tstv_count` stages, each `tstm_count` followed by that many `tst_type`/`tst_value` pairs (the members aggregate all elements; order of types within a stage varies). Present in 2964 delta states (1011 + 362 + 1591 with 27=0); the exact engine formula is `rgb * min(lighting + a, 1)`.
- **Cutout blends**: an `AlphaBlend` submesh (rst 27=1, opac 1) whose texture alpha is essentially 0/255 (≤ 2 % of texels in 16..239 — **a deliberate deviation from the engine for speed, threshold `CUTOUT_PARTIAL_MAX` is a guess**, not faithful behaviour) is emitted as `AlphaTest`: the engine blends it (z-write off, sorted) but the result equals a cutout, and avoids per-instance sorted blending. Done in `decode_record_into` (the texture is needed). 347 of 1225 blended submeshes in 1010001 became alpha-test; the remaining 886 have real soft alpha or opac < 1 and stay `AlphaBlend`.
- Not representable in the contract and dropped: `env_texture` (268 materials; only drawn when the device has ≥ 2 texture stages and the visual's env flag `+0xe1` is set), detail/secondary texture channels (`tst_*`), node `prio`, z-write state, `anim_matrix`/uv/visibility animation keys. Vertex colours: **no mesh uses them** — every `SimpleMesh` has FVF 0x112 (no diffuse/specular), and rst 145=0 takes the diffuse from the material anyway — so `Vertex::color` is white. `FvfLayout` still skips diffuse/specular bytes if another layout shows up.

**ICC pf 4582 shuttleport-ruins white statels (2026-10-14, user-reported, fixed)**: screenshot showed flat white "broken platform" slabs and dark angular debris sitting among correctly textured scenery (green shuttleport towers) on the beach-side ruins near `(930, 20, 755)`, plus a white rectangle across the own character's upper back (an `Attractor06_back` item). Reproduced offscreen (`aomac view pf 4582 --eye 895,45,-815 --at 906.2,38.5,-827.6`): a nearby low flat box and two small cubes rendered pure flat white next to two fully textured military crates, matching the screenshot. Those statels' mesh ids (229608/229609/229610, 205622, 250704, 42158/42161) have **zero** texture data anywhere in their own `ObjectArchive` (no node/material `delta_state` `tch_type`/`tch_text`, no `env_texture`, no `NewTextureData_t` override), confirmed by dumping the raw archive (`ao_formats::archive`); every *other* texture referenced by their neighbours decodes cleanly, ruling out a decode/format failure, a texture-quality mismatch, a statel-attribute parsing bug, or a GPU/bind-group regression.
An earlier pass of this investigation stopped at "the real client's D3D diffuse is white for an archive mesh with no texture" (the finding directly above) and closed this as faithful. That stopped short: **a `SimpleMesh` with no texture channel at all is not the same as a `SimpleMesh` whose diffuse is explicitly white** — the native renderer never *clears* texture stage 0 for it, it just never touches it, so the GPU keeps whatever the previous draw call bound. Found by decompiling the real per-draw apply path in **randy31.dll** (not DisplaySystem.dll, where the address a stale note cited resolves to an unrelated bounding-volume routine): `RVisual_t::ApplyStateChanges` (`@0x1004cbe1`, virtual, called once per node "before the mesh loop") and the material delta-state apply `FUN_10040aa0` (called unconditionally from `RViewPort_t::SetMaterial` `@0x1004b199`, *before* the opac/emis/spec copy already documented above) both do **nothing at all** when the node/material has no stored `RDeltaState` (`this+0xb8` / `this+0x70` null) — no render-state and no texture-stage call is issued. When a delta state *is* present, its texture-stage apply (`RDeltaState`'s vtable slot `+0x10`, `FUN_1002f275`) only calls `DeviceState::SetTexture` for a channel it actually authors and resolves (`tch_count` channels, each only if its resolved texture pointer is non-null); a channel-less delta state, or one whose referenced texture didn't load, skips `SetTexture` for that stage entirely. Either way the GPU's current diffuse binding survives into this submesh's draw, exactly the "flat white slab, textured crate right next to it" symptom.
**Fix** (`ao_formats::mesh::Builder::last_tex`, `playfield::Placer::last_diffuse`): the texture resolved for a `SimpleMesh` now falls back to the *previous* `SimpleMesh`'s effective texture (tracked through the archive's own node traversal, the same order the native loop visits them) instead of `None`, seeded for each statel placement from whatever the *previous statel's* last `SimpleMesh` left bound (`playfield::Placer`, in the existing global-then-per-zone placement order — a reasonable, documented approximation of the native draw order: within-archive order is exact, cross-statel order assumes the native opaque world list is drawn in the same file/zone order it's read in, not independently sorted/batched). A statel decode is only recomputed per preceding-texture context when it actually still has an unresolved submesh after that context is applied (`Placer::ctx_cache`); the overwhelming majority of meshes (every submesh textured on its own) are cached exactly as before, unaffected. `aomac view pf 4582` at the reproduction camera now shows the slab/cubes with the same rusted-metal crate texture as their neighbours instead of flat white (`crates/ao-formats/src/mesh.rs::tests::textureless_simple_mesh_inherits_the_caller_supplied_texture`). The same fix was extended to attached character meshes (`character::actor::ActorRig::new`): the body's own last resolved texture now seeds the head mesh, then each attachment in mount order (module doc: `meshes[0]` = body, then head, then attachments in the order given — the exact native `AddAttractorMesh` sequence), covered by `character::actor::tests::textureless_attachment_inherits_the_body_last_texture` (a known-blank world-debris mesh mounted on `Attractor06_back` for the test; real retail items that genuinely have no texture channel will now inherit instead of drawing white).
**Follow-up (2026-10-14): the own character's white back item is still unidentified.** The user's Wear window shows a white/silver stick-like icon in UTILS 1; the obvious candidate from a real capture is `summon_light` (mesh **26163**), seen mounted on `Attractor06_back` (place 5) on the player `Bergdoktor` in `docs/captures/zone_ithaca.rec` (`docs/zone/dynel.md` §1.3). Rendering that exact capture (`aomac` test harness, `AOMAC_SHOT_DIR`, `play::dynels::variant_tests::captured_remote_appearance_screenshots`) reproduces a flat-looking white bar across the upper back, visually matching the report — **but it is not a texture-resolution bug**: both of mesh 26163's submeshes resolve real, decoded, non-degenerate textures (1010004/41647, a light cream/metal 16×64 image, mean RGB ≈ (229,233,220); 1010004/5949, a dark brown 128×128 image, mean RGB ≈ (74,57,49)), confirmed pixel-for-pixel in `built.model.textures` for the live-captured Bergdoktor build, and the rendered bar shows visible shading/texture grain under magnification, not a solid `#ffffff` fill. At normal human-scale screen size a light-cream, minified item texture reads as "white" faithfully, independent of this fix. So `summon_light` is a legitimate glow item rendering as authored, not the reported defect; the user's actual equipped item remains unidentified (their character/inventory is out of scope to inspect live). The attachment-inheritance fix above is still correct and real (any attached mesh that genuinely has no texture channel, world debris or a worn item, now inherits instead of drawing white) — it just doesn't happen to be what's visible in this specific capture.
**Resolution (2026-10-15): the item is "Light Bar" and the real bug was a missing feature, not a texture.** The user identified their UTILS 1 item by name: "Light Bar", confirming the `summon_light` (mesh 26163) candidate above, and that it "should illuminate the character's surroundings during night" — a feature gap, not the texture-resolution bug this section is otherwise about. `ao_formats::mesh::decode_mesh_lights` (already used for the login/creation screens) decodes mesh 26163's own `RLight_t` node directly: `pos (0, 0.71, 0)` relative to the item, colour `(1.0, 0.836, 0.394)`, range `6.13 m`, atten `(0, 0.163, 0)` — a warm point light, never applied to the live world: `character::actor::ActorRig::new` decoded the head/attachment meshes themselves but never their embedded lights, and nothing fed a *moving* light into the renderer at all (`ao-render`'s `LightGrid` is a static grid built once per scene upload, not per frame).
**Fix**: `Mount::lights` (`character/actor.rs`) decodes each mounted mesh's embedded lights once at load; `ActorRig::mount_lights(parts)` places them by the mount's current pose (actor-relative). `aomac/play/dynels.rs`'s per-frame character loop transforms them by the dynel's own world transform and pushes them into a new `Host::dynamic_lights` (the same per-frame "push every frame, forgotten if not pushed" convention as `Host::actors`). `ao-render` gained a second, small, per-frame-rewritten storage buffer (`Renderer::set_dynamic_lights`, `MAX_DYN_LIGHTS` = 16) bound alongside the static light grid's buffers; `shader.wgsl`'s `vertex_lights` (used by terrain, statics and actors alike) now sums both the static grid cell *and* this per-frame list with the identical D3D7 attenuation/spot formula, sharing the native 8-light-per-vertex budget — and runs the dynamic loop unconditionally (not gated behind the static grid's bounds check), so it also lights indoor/edge points the static grid has no cell for. A dropped dynel's light is dropped the same frame (nothing lingers once it stops being pushed).
Verified: `character::actor::tests::textureless_attachment_inherits_the_body_last_texture` (synthetic, the in-archive texture fix); `play::dynels::variant_tests::worn_light_bar_reaches_the_renderer_as_a_tracked_point_light` (real capture, Bergdoktor's real `summon_light` mount: asserts a dynamic light appears near his tracked position with the authored range/colour once his model is ready, and disappears the frame his dynel is removed). Opt-in visual check (`AOMAC_SHOT_DIR`, `play::dynels::variant_tests::captured_light_bar_lights_the_ground_at_night`): a dark/near-zero-ambient scene with Bergdoktor and a plain ground plane, rendered with the dynamic light off and on — off: he's a dim ambient silhouette; on: his vest/pants/boots are visibly lit in the light's warm colour from behind, confirming it reaches *other* actor geometry, not just the glowing item's own emissive submesh.
**Own-avatar cutover (2026-10-15, bug 8):** the preceding world loop covered remote dynels only; `Player::frame` did not submit the own avatar's mounted lights. The initial cutover appended `Avatar::lights()` outside the body visibility/fade branch; the visibility correction below supersedes that placement. The avatar shares its retained composed pose between light placement and body drawing, and applies the same translation, heading and body scale as its actor transform to light positions (and the linear transform to spotlight directions). Light colour, attenuation and range remain authored, as in the remote path. Retail evidence: Randy31 `RLight_t` constructor **0x1003fe81**, `Process` **0x1003ff72** registers frame-tree lights each frame; `RVisual_t::CullLights` **0x1004ce96** has no own/remote distinction. Source data: mesh **1010001/26163**, mounted at back place **5** in Bergdoktor's captured `zone_ithaca.rec`; no guessed replacement light is introduced.
Historical verification of the original own-avatar cutover used the captured own avatar and real mesh 26163 on a synthetic back mount: posed/scaled world position, authored photometry, zero-alpha body, movement/heading and same-frame unequip. Its old hidden-first-person emission assertion was incorrect; the replacement regression is documented below. The prior opt-in offscreen check (`AOMAC_AUDIO_MUTE=1 AOMAC_SHOT_DIR=/tmp/World6/own-light-shots cargo test --release -p aomac play::player::tests::own_light_bar_lights_the_ground_at_night -- --exact --nocapture`) wrote `own-light-bar-{close,wide}-{off,on,hidden}.png` at **640×480** and **5120×1440**. Those images established the authored warm light pool, but their hidden-body pool represented the old implementation, not faithful visibility behavior.
Unlit first-person avatars retain the old no-skinning path: `ActorRig::has_mount_lights()` gates pose refresh during light collection. Verified `play::avatar::tests::unlit_hidden_avatar_does_not_sample_a_pose_for_lights` checks that collecting an unlit avatar's lights leaves its skin absent and its deferred body-pose refresh pending. Workspace release tests passed (1824 tests, 60 ignored), as did workspace all-target release clippy with warnings denied.
**First-person visibility gate resolved (2026-10-15):** the earlier hidden-body emission above was incorrect and is superseded. N3 `n3VisualDynel_t::Run` **0x100196bd** reads `ShowMyCharacter` only for the client-control dynel; false plus camera mode 0 (`GetMode` **0x1002005e**, `IsFirstPerson` **0x10020071**) calls DisplaySystem `VisualCATMesh_t::DisableVisibility` **0x10073693**. This removes the CAT frame from its parent through vslot +0x14 and hides its attractor meshes; `EnableVisibility` **0x10073628** adds the frame back through `RandyRoot_t::AddToRoot` **0x1006aee6**. Randy `RViewPort_t::Process` **0x1004bf39** processes the root tree; CAT frame Process **0x1005798b** reaches `RCATMesh_t::ProcessAttractorChilds` **0x100577d3** (mode 1), which invokes each mounted frame's Process, ultimately registering embedded lights at **0x1003ff72**. A detached CAT frame cannot take this path. `Player::frame` therefore submits mounted lights inside the existing `Camera3p::show_avatar()` branch, independent of body alpha but not visibility. All helper callers were traced: body/pick submission and `prepare_effect_source`'s visibility input already use that same gate and remain unchanged. Regression `own_light_bar_tracks_visible_player_frame_and_unequips` checks zero-alpha third person, hidden first-person suppression, immediate restoration with `ShowMyCharacter`, movement/heading/scale, authored photometry and same-frame unequip. The opt-in offscreen screenshot fixture now suppresses the hidden light too; prior hidden-view bright-ground results above describe the superseded behavior, not retail parity. Fixtures retain the captured own head (place 0, flags 4) with the synthetic Light Bar mount (place 5, flags 0, matching Bergdoktor's capture).
Native callsite cross-check: Randy's only code caller of `ProcessAttractorChilds` **0x100577d3** is CAT Process **0x1005798b**. DisplaySystem's import **0x10089658** has two callers: `UpdateRandyAttractors` **0x10072a23** and `SetScale` **0x10072bbc**; both pass mode **0**, which only updates visibility/transforms and does not invoke mounted Process. The hidden frame therefore has no alternate light-registration route through those helpers. The detach vslot resolves to Randy `RRefFrame_t::RemoveChild` **0x10044f10**, which unlinks the child and clears its parent/sibling links.
Verification of this visibility correction: `AO_CLIENT_DIR=~/Games/ProjectRubiKa/client AOMAC_AUDIO_MUTE=1 CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/GapLightBar/target cargo test --release --workspace -j 2` passed, including `own_light_bar_tracks_visible_player_frame_and_unequips` against the installed retail assets; `cargo clippy --release --workspace --all-targets -j 2 -- -D warnings` passed. Headless only: no window, desktop input, screenshots or live login.

### Results
`cargo run --release -p ao-formats --example mesh_stats` (all ids):
- 1010001: 9925 records → **9925 decoded** (9906 with geometry: 7 785 496 vertices, 7 398 795 triangles; 19 contain only animation/helper nodes: lights, collision spheres, connectors), 0 failures. 31 136 merged submeshes (emissive and glow-mask split merge groups): opaque 28 832, alpha-test 1418, alpha-blend 886 (after the cutout conversion above; 1071 / 1225 before), additive 0 (no 19/20 states in the data), two-sided 1692 (5 %); 733 untextured submeshes, 653 of them with a non-white `diff` in the data (0 textured ones) — not drawn tinted: the client's D3D diffuse is white, see *Vertex lighting* (the loader's `base_color` is white for all of them).
- 1010026: 699 / 699 decoded, 321 410 vertices, 224 910 triangles; 2310 submeshes: opaque 2032, alpha-test 252, alpha-blend 26, additive 0, two-sided 82; 14 untextured (8 with a non-white `diff`, drawn white).
- Emissive / glow (mesh_stats): 1010001 emissive submeshes 2447, glow-mask 2824 (both 144); 1010026 67 / 354 (19). Glowing mesh ids: emissive (1,1,1) 1415, 1419, 1436, 1442, 3146, 5968, 6113; glow-mask windows 17541, 21397 (orange lit windows), 10918. Seen in the viewer: Omni-1 (705) bright strip lights on the towers, Newland City (566) cyan neon street sign and cyan building lights.
- Visual check (`aomac view mesh <id> --screenshot`): 1418 and 22854 (buildings, upright, windows textured), 6273 (tree: trunk + alpha-cut leaves), 6306 (desert tree), 1535 (rock), 3480 (barrels, logo un-mirrored), 3722 (building with lattice masts via alpha test), 13937 (lamp), 29216 (alpha ivy), 41920 (banner with correct dragon art), 36128 (house).; materials (after the renderer's blend/cull/colour support landed): 6616 (hovercraft), 9918 (flat plane; its pink `diff` is not drawn, see *Vertex lighting*), 11007 (translucent magenta ring: opac 0.5 blend), 2175 (35 % translucent column with a magenta flat-colour object), 3722 (culled walls, alpha-blended windows, cutout lattice masts two-sided).
- Tests: `archive::tests`, `mesh::tests` (hand-built byte fixtures incl. an end-to-end quad with `anim_matrix`), `tests/mesh_real.rs` (skips without the client).

## playfields
Code: `crates/ao-formats/src/playfield.rs` + `playfield/{record,ground,statel}.rs`. Everything below was derived from Ghidra decompiles of the original client DLLs (N3.dll, DisplaySystem.dll, Gamecode.dll, GameData.dll; addresses are the 32-bit image addresses of the PRK 0.6.1 client) plus data inspection; no community source was needed.

### Record types (rdb `rdb_<type>(id, version, data)`; all keyed by playfield id unless noted)
| type | content | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1000001 | playfield resource `RDBPlayfield_t` (name, tilemap id, zone/room list) | `ReadBlob` N3 @0x1001c115; 601 ids = the playfield set |
| 1000003 | **statel file** (placed static meshes) | `n3StatelController_t::OpenStatelFile` @0x100253d4 requests `Identity{0xf4243=1000003, playfield id}`; 600 ids |
| 1000009 | tilemap `RDBTilemap_t`: magic `CHGA` = outdoor heightfield, `GNDA` = dungeon template atlas | `n3TilemapController_t::GetTilemap` @0x10017ff8 requests `Identity{0xf4249=1000009, tilemap id}`; 325 CHGA + 111 GNDA records |
| 1010006 / 1010021 / 1010022 | ground tile textures 256² / 128² / 64² (24 zero bytes, then a JPEG (417) or an RGBA PNG (432)) | `FUN_10036180` returns 0xf6956/0xf6965/0xf6966 by quality (DisplaySystem @0x10036150); all 849 ids exist |
| 1010001 | static mesh referenced by statels | every statel mesh id of playfields 566/705/120 is in 1010001 (see `## meshes`) |
| 1000046 / 1000047 | 28 byte records `f32 centre xyz, f32 radius` = bounding sphere per mesh id | inspected (not needed for loading) |
| 1000014 | district name table | [INFERENCE: mapping from "District" content] |
| 1000029 | native `PlayfieldAreaInfo_t`: area names, EnvironmentData, AO centres, point lists and zone-to-area mapping (`ao_formats::playfield::area`) | GameData `ReadBlob` 0x10008ed2, `AreaData` reader 0x100032e4, `GetAreaData(zone)` 0x10008cb8; GC resource factory 0x1011eeb4 uses `proxy.playfield.instance`, not geometry's `exit_door_id.instance` |
| 1000007 | **baked ground shadow maps** (six 8 bit PNGs per playfield, see *Terrain rendering*) | same layout as `data/Shadows/<id>.sdw`; 323 ids [INFERENCE: the type was labelled "map/minimap PNG" before; the content is the sdw format, the DLL consumer was not found by name] |
| 1000008 | land-control bitmap per playfield (`ao_formats::landcontrol`): `u32 ver, u16 w, u16 h, 12 B`, then `h` rows of `ceil(w/8)` bytes, LSB = left; 53 ids, all `len == 20+ceil(w/8)*h` | INFERENCE from `MapData_c` → `N3Msg_GetLandControlBitmap`; earlier "PNG sets" label was wrong (no PNG inside) |
`data/Statels/<id>.pf` are **stale copies** of 1000003 (2003/2004 timestamps): 368 of 473 are byte-identical to the rdb record, 105 differ; the engine reads the rdb record. `data/Shadows/<id>.sdw` (244 files) are older copies of rdb 1000007.

**AreaInfo / Grid identity.** `1000029` has no eight-byte RDB prefix: `u16 version=6,
u32 zone_count, u8 area_count`, then `area_count` version-4 AreaData records and
`zone_count` one-byte area indices. AreaData contains four packed NUL strings
(name, owner, description, knowledge), raw 26-byte EnvironmentData in stream
order, two control percentages, two level bounds, seven words, an AO-space
centre, and two counted point lists. The final two words are default/allowed
building-style masks; the first five meanings remain unresolved. Point factory
GameData 0x10002abd reads base position/flags (0x10001eca), ACG name/styles
(types 1–3, 0x10001295), fence rotation (7, 0x100025a9), placement angles
(8, 0x100028af), then each point's zone. No weather radius is serialized.
Environment decoding reuses the playfield EnvironmentData reader without its
12-byte header; weather blending is not implemented by this parser.

`N3Msg_IsGrid` GC **0x10121d84** resolves **zone 0 through the zone-to-area
mapping**, then compares its name exactly against `"Grid"` (0x1016fd14,
bytes `47 72 69 64 00`; little-endian word `0x64697247`). It does not inspect
the first area, a geometry id, or a hand-maintained Grid id table. Native
SetAreaInfo is GC 0x101215c1. `Zone.area_playfield` preserves the proxy identity
before the exit-door geometry remap; the world loader publishes `Zone.is_grid`
from that resource, defaults missing metadata to native false, and propagates
malformed resources through the normal failed-world-load path.

The installed survey covers **600 records: 594 native-valid / 1998 areas and
six native-rejected resources**. All valid records have exactly four packed
strings. All 600 records end with 12 zero bytes after the native-read boundary;
native does not inspect trailing bytes or require EOF, so neither does the parser.
The six 19-byte zero-count resources (1424, 1673, 2006, 2014, 2024, 4352) fail
the native nonzero-count requirement and return parser errors, not fabricated
empty metadata. Survey Grid names occur only in 152 and 4107; these are regression
expectations, not runtime permission tables.
PF560 demonstrates why mapping matters: area 0 is `Mort Crater`, while zone 0
maps to area 3, `Czeffyr Desert`. `playfield_real` retains the complete survey
and nine point-type counts; parser unit tests cover zone-zero indirection and
malformed/truncated data.


### Playfield record (1000001)
```
u32 version (7..10)  u32 id  char name[32]
u32 tilemap id (rdb 1000009)  u32 zone size in tiles (10)  u32 count (zones, or rooms for dungeons)
if version > 8: u32 resource_flags(+50), u32(+54), u32 vehicle_flags(+58), u32(+5c), u32(+60), 0x18 bytes
if tilemap id != id: count x room            (dungeon / indoor)
else (outdoor): count x { u32 n, n x camera attractor (vec3 + quat + vec3 + f32) }
room := u8 flags (rot = flags&3, 0x80 = has name)  u8
        u16 x1,z1,x2,z2 (tile rect in the dungeon tilemap)  f32 pos[3] (room centre, world)
        u16 ndoors, ndoors x (u16,u16)   [char name[32] if flags&0x80]
        i32 lightmap size+4, i32 present, [size bytes]
        u32 (n+1)*0x3f1 liquid blocks: n x { u32, u32 n1, n1 x vec3, u32 n2, n2 x 3 u16 }
        if version > 4: u32 n, n x (vec3 + quat + [vec3 if version>=6] + f32)   camera attractors
```
(`n3Room_t` reader N3 @0x10012803.) Rule found empirically over all 601 records: **tilemap id == playfield id ⇔ outdoor heightfield playfield** (325 records; for 324 the statel zones use the outdoor layout, 4622 has its own heightfield but dungeon-style zones); the other 276 are dungeons/indoor playfields built from rooms. After the zones (outdoor: per zone `u32 n` + `n × 44` byte attractors; dungeon: after the rooms) every record has the `RDBPlayfieldAnarchy_t` tail: liquids + environment, see *Liquids* and *Environment* (`Record::tail` = its offset).

Native vehicle permission: `RDBPlayfield_t::ReadBlob` (N3 `1001c115`) reads those five words from blob offsets52/56/60/64/68 into runtime offsets`+50/+54/+58/+5c/+60`; constructor N3 `1001bf0e` initializes all five to zero (also the value for versions7/8). `PlayfieldAnarchy_t::IsShadowlandPF` (GC `10121906`) tests resource_flags bit1. `AreVehiclesAllowed` (GC `10121915`) requires runtime byte`+5b` bit0 clear, hence vehicle_flags`&0x01000000 == 0`, and not Shadowlands. `ao_formats::playfield::vehicles_allowed` reads the real resource and errors on absence/malformed data; it never assumes permission. Regression `native_vehicle_resource_and_shadowland_refusals` covers both refusals, old-version constructor defaults, and the distinction between the high-byte permission bit and low bit.

### Heightfield tilemap (1000009, `CHGA`)
```
"CHGA" u32 size u32 version(=1) u16 width u16 height   valid size in CELLS (Newland City 150x150)
f32 cell size (4.0 m) f32 height scale (0.2)
u16 n; n x u16 tile index -> ground texture id (1010006/21/22)
u32 (=1), then a Funcom object archive (same member encoding as `## meshes`, but without the version/range header):
  u32 name_count, name_count x {cstr kind, cstr name}, u32 object_count, (object_count+1) x object
  object 1 = AnarchyGroundData: map_width, map_height (vertices = cells+1 padded to whole patches),
  map_modulo, tiletexture_count, heightmap_compressed_data, heightmap_small_data,
  tilemap_compressed_data, [buildingmap_compressed_data], texture refs, tile_type_data
```
*Patches.* `m = min(2^tz(map_width-1), 2^tz(map_height-1), 64)` (`tz` = trailing zeros; DisplaySystem @0x10036f17 and @0x10035966); a patch is `m×m` cells with `(m+1)²` vertices; `(map_width-1)/m × (map_height-1)/m` patches, row-major. The three `*_data` members are concatenations of `u32 length + bytes`, one entry per patch.
*Heights* (`Patch_t::DecompressHeightMap` @0x10035df0): zlib stream of `(m+1)²` bytes (when `heightmap_small_data` is 4 bytes) or `(m+1)²` 16-bit residuals (8 bytes = four cached corner heights). The samples are the **second order prefix sum** of the stream — first running down every column, then along every row, wrapping at8/16 bits. Byte samples expand with`<<8` (`10035db8`). The final sample is **unsigned**: N3`10017c5c` executes `MOVZX EAX,AX`, then `FILD`/`FMUL header_scale` and stores a float (`10017c62..68`). `10017bb9` shifts the expanded sample`>>8` only for the byte family. The port's normalized `height_scale` representation gives the same metres. All22 wide maps retain their stored pre-divided scale; no signed reinterpretation, base-height subtraction or fitted translation applies. The historical statel-origin/terrain median gaps (including4364/4380–4388) are diagnostics, not a native floor-contact constraint: authored statel origins need not lie on terrain. See the native-coordinate proof below.
*Tiles* (`tilemap_compressed_data`): zlib `m×m` u16 per patch. The tile index is `value & mask` with **mask 0x3fff if the archive has a non-empty `tile_type_data` member, else 0xff** (ctor sets `+0x60`; verified: 325/325 CHGA records have all indices < n with this rule, 182 would violate it with a fixed 0x3fff). Index → texture id via the header `u16` list; every cell is one textured quad (the engine bakes per-patch textures from the same tiles, `FUN_10037957`). Outdoor geometry uses checkerboard parity `(~z ^ x) & 1` (N3 `FUN_10017800`), not the raw tile orientation bits. The separate indoor room-surface triangle path uses bit 14 (`n3RoomSurface_t::GetTileTriangles` @0x10014888).
*World mapping*: AO x = column × 4 m, z = row × 4 m (statels sit on the terrain with this mapping: all 1090 statels of 566 lie in x −41..592, z 0..599 for a 150-cell = 600 m map). Scene space negates z (see `## meshes`).
