**Lightmap oracle (shell vertex count check).** Each room record stores the vertex count the client's `CreateDungeonRoom` produced when the lightmap was baked (`Room.lightmap.0`, see *Room lightmaps*), so the number of shell vertices after welding is checkable per room: `cargo test --release -p ao-formats dungeon_lightmap_survey -- --ignored --nocapture`. Over all 601 playfields (2000 rooms with a lightmap): **1863 → 1875 → 1991 matching (93.2 % → 93.8 % → 99.55 %)**. What the oracle found:
* *Ceiling stretch is a signed byte* (+116 rooms, every room with a non-flat `HCDA` layer: before, "floor flat/var, ceil var" matched 133/143 and 191/303; after 140/143 and 300/303; rooms with only floor relief 508/508). `HCDA` values in the data are 0…37 and 242…255. The loader `FUN_10077e6f` (DisplaySystem @0x10077e6f) adds 0x20 to each byte (`*p = *p + ' '` on `uchar`, wrapping) and the warp `FUN_10001c3b` subtracts 0x20 from the unsigned byte again, so the stretch is `((raw + 0x20) & 0xff) − 0x20` ∈ −32…223 (`dungeon::stretch`); we had used the unsigned raw value (242…255 → +48…51 m). It only mattered for the welding because shared-edge normals/positions of stretched ceilings no longer agreed.
* *Welding arithmetic* (+12 rooms; `FUN_10001645` disassembly, `weld.asm`): the constants `DAT_1003c890`/`c898` are **doubles holding the floats 0.1/0.2** (bytes `00 00 00 a0 99 99 b9 3f` / `… c9 3f`), `DAT_1003c8a0` the float 0.001, `DAT_1003c8a8` the double 30. `(cand.uv − v.uv) + 0.1` is one rounding to float (`FSTP float`), its fraction `t − floor(t)` is compared `<= 0.2f`; pass 2 compares `|off − (cand.uv − v.uv)|` (one rounding) against 0.1f with the vertex's **unshifted** uv (the offset is only added to the vertices that are kept); the distances are sums `z²+x²+y²` of float differences. Door frames use uv steps of 0.05/0.95/1.05, so exact ties (`1.05 − 0.95 + 0.1 = 0.19999996`) decide merges; the earlier all-f32 version resolved some wrongly (piece 242 door frame: +1 vertex in every use). 24 bit FPU precision (Direct3D default) was tried and matches fewer rooms (1969), 53/64 bit is what the data fits. The hash prefilter `((H+0x10101)|0x808080) − H' & 0x7c7c7c == 0` accepts position hash differences −2…+1 per axis and never rejects a pair within 0.0316 m (verified: emulating it changes nothing). Candidates never include the earlier vertices of the *same* block (tried: 1295/2000).
* *Unmatched wall keys*: the client's key table (`FUN_10017306` N3 @0x10017306: for each of the 128 states, min rotation of `state&0xf` OR `state&0x70` searched in `DAT_1005bce0`; rotation extras all 0) yields byte 0 when the key is not in `KEYS`, and `FUN_10016967` then reads the word *before* the row (`+0x23a`, the table starts at `+0x23c`), i.e. slot 15 of the previous row; `Gnda::cell_mesh` does the same. No cell of the shipped data hits it (door frame states are only ever `0x11/0x31/0x51/0x71` on a canonical-1 cell, and `0x12/14/18` rotations), so "door frame whose key is not in KEYS" never occurs in the data (earlier assumption that a door on a 2-wall cell lacks its piece was wrong: no such state exists).
* *Cells with mesh word 0* (4604:0, 4833:0, 4885:0/5; tile 2/12, keys `05`/`07` whose table words are 0 in the data; 10/4/63/14 such cells, all 1-wide corridor stubs). The disassembly says the client draws **nothing** there: `FUN_10001a91` (N3 @0x10001a91) ends with `AND EDI,0x3fff` / `JZ 0x10001c28` right after reading the cell word (@0x10001af9), skipping the piece lookup, the floor extra (`[this+0x14]`) and the corner mask (`[this+0x1c]`); `FUN_10001c3b` additionally returns when `[this+0xc]` is null. `FUN_100168f6` stores `(rot<<14)|mesh` unchanged, `FUN_10016967` returns the table word (0 here), no TAHA entry applies (all four rooms have none). Yet skipping those cells makes these rooms miss exactly one flat floor quad per cell (deltas −18, −8, −128, −28 = 1.8…2.0 vertices per cell after welding) and keeping the floor piece reproduces the lightmap count exactly (corner posts are irrelevant there: removing them changes nothing). The port therefore draws the flat floor of the tile on those cells (visible floor instead of a hole). **UNRESOLVED**: the disassembly of the constructor does not explain where the baked vertex count of these four rooms got its floor from (the baked lightmaps could come from an older build of the tool; not verifiable).
* *The 7 tie-level rooms* (321:55/56/57, 1183:2, 1184:1, 1185:1 = +1; 322:0 = −1) were attacked from the arithmetic side and it is exhausted: every compare of `FUN_10001645` (pass 1 `floor(c−v+0.1)`/`≤0.2`, seen-table with the "different vertex" repeat rule, last-entry fallback, pass 2 `|off−(c−v)|≤0.1`, SWAR position hash, candidate ranges incl. the `param_3 == param_8·0x24+param_5` special case which changes nothing) was re-derived from the disassembly and matches the port; variants (24 bit FPU rounding of any subset of the four operation groups: 1969–1986, first instead of last offset: 1490, looser/tighter uv thresholds by ±1e-8…1e-7: 1857–1990, hash prefilter emulation: 1991, candidate ring variants: no change) never beat 1991. In 321:55 the whole difference sits in the ceiling block of the east door cell (4,2) (`cur 14`): the vertex `(177.9,37,−204)` has uv `(1.0, 0.050000012)` (piece 97, `0x3d4cccd0`) and its neighbour in the cell above uv `(−4.9995, 0.95)`; pass 1 finds the offset `(−6, 1)` through `0.9f + 0.1f = 0.99999998 → 1.0f`, pass 2 then rejects the very same pair (`|1 − 0.9f| = 0.10000002 > 0.1f`), so the block keeps 4 new vertices where the lightmap has 3. The identical numeric tie decides 124:0–3 and ~40 other rooms the other way, so no single global threshold fits; the +1 rooms are copies of one module family. **UNRESOLVED**: input difference not found.
* *The 2 TAHA rooms* (124:6 = −24, 127:42 = +3). TAHA is decoded fully per `FUN_10077d11` (DisplaySystem @0x10077d11: `u16 k`, k × `{u8 tile_index, u8 count, count × u16 mesh}` stored at `table + tile·4 + 0x28`, then the PNG) and `FUN_100169ab` @0x1001720c…: the per-cell byte `b` selects `list[tile][b>>2 − 1]` instead of the autotiled mesh when `b>>2 != 0` (rotation stays that of the autotile state), `b&1` is the door-frame side hint read for the neighbour cells (case table @0x10016d00…0x10016dd2: ported literally), `b&3 == 2` forces plain floor (`local_8 = 0` after the corner claim). Without any override only 1501 of 2000 rooms match (124:6 is then +78), with them 124:6 is −24. In 127:42 switching off the single override at (44,11) (list entry 519) does not change the count at all, so the +3 is not caused by TAHA there but by the same kind of weld decision as above; for 124:6 switching off any single override moves the count by 1…6 (not 24). **UNRESOLVED**: no concrete cause found.

**Gaps.** Not ported: liquids, per-tile `b` random floor variants (never present; key variants `1d/33` are unreachable). Room textures use the highest mip set (1010009/1010004). Ceilings are drawn (two-sided renderer): from above, dungeon interiors are hidden — fly inside (`aomac view pf <id> --eye … --at …`). Screenshots checked after the fixes: playfield 124 room 0 (door frame, piece 242, in the end wall of a corridor, lit floor strips), 341 room 67 (cave room, door frame piece 119 in the rock wall, stretched ceiling).

## characters
Code: `crates/ao-formats/src/character.rs` + `character/{cat,anim}.rs`; survey `cargo run --release -p ao-formats --example char_stats [-- --list]`; viewer `aomac view char <id> [--anim <id> --time <s>] [--head <1010001 id>]`. Ground truth: Ghidra decompiles of `randy31.dll` (PE base 0x10000000; "CAT" = the engine's skinned-mesh library, statically linked there): `CATMesh_t::CATMesh_t(DataIO_t*,vector<string>const&)` @10052ff8 (mesh reader), `CATKeyframeAnimData_t::CATKeyframeAnimData_t(DataIO_t*)` @10052068 (animation reader), `RMaterial_t::RMaterial_t` @10041043, `CATRender_t::SetAnim` @10053fe2, bone-matrix recursion `FUN_100540a5`, skinning loops `FUN_100543bc` / `FUN_1005470d`, key lookup `FUN_10051d2a` (rotation) / `FUN_10051df4` (translation), slerp `FUN_10053d0b`, delta key streams `FUN_1005bb82` (ctor) / `FUN_1005b96a` (reader) / `FUN_10051c9d` (factory). Everything is `DataIO_t` little-endian: vtable slots +0x14 u32, +0x1c u16, +0x24 i8, +0x2c f32, +0x34 string (`u32 len` + bytes). No community documentation was used.

### Record types
- `rdb_1010002` (770): a complete skinned model = texture table + `CATMesh` stream (skeleton, materials, weighted vertices). Player bodies (ids 5900 athrox m, 5907 solitus m, 5914 opifex m, 5921 nanomage m, 5927 solitus f, 5934 opifex f, 5941 nanomage f – named by their `*_<race><sex>_default.png` textures; later ids add trenchcoat/armour variants), NPCs, creatures, mechs, chairs. 167 distinct skeletons.
- `rdb_1010027` (8): same container and ids as eight 1010002 towers (`maintower`, `Bone01_Main`, …), ~3× fewer triangles — the low-detail proxy, as 1010026 is for 1010001. (Its 32-byte name field holds the root-bone name followed by float garbage, like the others.)
- `rdb_1010003` (3190): one animation clip each; fits every model whose skeleton hash is equal (898 clips fit the human `Bip01_ac` skeleton, hash `0xbd945cbe`). The clip record carries no name, but the client's name table (below) does: 3168 of 3190 clips are named `<set>_<role>_01_01.ani` (`athrox_walk_01_01.ani` = 10078, `male_idle-stand_01_01.ani` = 10173). Example: 9382 is `athrox_sneakcool` (2.5 s, right foot at 600 ms, left at 1100 ms), **not** the walk as earlier notes had it.
- Heads, hair and other attachments are **not** in these records: they are ordinary static meshes (`rdb_1010001`, see § meshes) mounted on an attractor, see below.

### Model record (1010002 / 1010027)
```
char[32] root          NUL-terminated root-bone name, rest is padding/garbage (0xCD or floats)
u32 marker             (parts + 1) * 1009
parts x { char[32] material_name; u32 texture; u32 env_texture; u32 alpha_aux }   44 bytes, same order/names as the materials
u32 ?                  varies (0x05010500, 0xffffffff, …); not read by the stream reader, ignored
CATMesh stream:
  u32 4 (magic), u32 version = 0x104 (0x67 also accepted by the engine, has no bind positions; absent in the data)
  u32 signature        skeleton hash, compared with the animation's by CATRender_t::SetAnim @10053fe2
  f32 ×2               unused
  u32 n_mat × { string name; u32 flags; string texture_file; [string env_file if flags & 2]; f32 ×15 }
  u32 n × f32 ×4       header-level records (always 0)
  u32 n_bone × { string name; f32 scale; u32 n_child; u32 child[] }        bone 0 is "-nobone-"; hierarchy via child lists
  u32 n_group × { string name ("-noselgroup-");
       u32 n_sub × { u32 material; u32 n_vert × vertex(68 B); u32 n_idx; u16 idx[] }      triangle list, D3D clockwise-front
       u32 n × { f32 x y z radius; u32 bone }                                              collision spheres
       u32 n × { string name; f32 ×8 (pos xyz, quat xyzw, scale); u32 bone } }             attractors
  trailer: 12 zero bytes (769/778 records) or a progressive-mesh LOD table (`vertex_reorder`, `indices`, `neighbours`, `num_splits`, `splits`, `catindices`; 9 records), ignored
vertex = { f32 local0[3]; f32 local1[3]; f32 bind[3]; f32 normal[3]; f32 uv[2]; u32 bone0; u32 bone1; f32 weight }
```
- **Skinning** (`FUN_100543bc`): `pos = w·(M[bone0]·local0) + (1−w)·(M[bone1]·local1)` with `M` the animated 3×4 world matrix of the bone; for `w` above a constant (~0.99) only `bone0`. The stored normal is **bone-local** to `bone0`: `n = R[bone0]·normal`. `bind` is the position in the bind pose; the file has no bind matrices, but `bind = R·local0 + t` holds exactly for every vertex fully weighted to a bone (residual 0 on all bones with ≥3 non-collinear such vertices), so `character::bind_frames` recovers each bone's bind frame from a 3-point frame fit and the bind-pose normals are `R_bind·normal`. Verified: 796 of 798 test triangles of 5900 have the CCW geometric normal agreeing with `R_bind·normal` (raw normals: 370 of 798).
- **Bone matrices** (`FUN_100540a5`): `world = parent ∘ (R(q), scale·t)` with the standard column-vector quaternion matrix (the engine stores its transpose for D3D row vectors; verified: the fitted bind local rotations match the animation's `R(q)`, not its transpose or conjugate, mean 15° over 60 arbitrary clips and 4.7° for the best one) and `scale` = product of the ancestors' `Bone::scale` (1.0 for humans; opifex 5914 has 0.77/0.91/1.50 on root/pelvis/spine, i.e. the shared human clips are re-proportioned by the mesh). The bind-pose translation of every bone equals the animation's constant translation to 4 decimals (e.g. Spine3 −0.1731, 0.0002, 0).
- Bones without a track in the clip keep identity rotation and zero offset (the engine's defaults).
- **Rest lengths differ per clip set** (found via `aomac view char 41664 --anim 9375`: forearms/hands/feet float away, bind pose fine). `FUN_100540a5` (decompiled again, @100540a5) applies the clip's translations literally: `t_local = anim_t · accumulated Bone::scale`; bone modes 1/2 in the engine's per-bone table (`this+0x20`) are controller hooks (default 0), not data. Clips of the same skeleton **hash** need not share bone lengths: the three sets `athrox`, `male`, `female` carry their own, e.g. for skeleton_solitus (41664, name table) the bind UpperArm offset is 0.118 m against 0.166 m in `athrox_*` clips (bind and clip offsets agree to 5 digits only within the model's own set: 5907/`male` 0.26302 vs 0.26302). So a model must be posed with its own set (`character_animations`/`role_anim`; models no set is named after, such as `skeleton_solitus`, get the set with the smallest `pose_detachment`, here `male`). `pose_detachment(store, id, anim, t)` = max over parent/child bone pairs of (nearest distance between the two bones' rigid vertices posed − in bind): 0.005–0.05 m with the right set (5900/`athrox` 9375 0.005; 5907/10191 0.014; 41664/10191 0.033), 0.13–0.18 m with another set (41664 + 9375/9382/10078 0.13, 5907 + athrox clips 0.16). A bind-length retarget factor per bone (`|bind offset|/|clip offset|`) was tried and dropped: it fixes the cross-set pose (0.17 → 0.03) but breaks Atrox (0.005 → 0.08) because several fitted frames are exact for their vertices without being the joint, and it is not what the client does. char_stats prints the metric over 763 models with their own set: worst 0.357 m (hellhound 29902, a large animal; screenshot intact), 16 above 0.15 m, all large creatures/wing flappers that were inspected as intact (29902, 16777).
- **Materials** (`RMaterial_t` ctor @10041043; float order = `FAFMaterial_t` members): diffuse, specular, ambient, emissive RGB, shininess, shininess strength, opacity. `flags & 8` ⇒ the texture's alpha channel is *not* transparency (`IsAlphaUsedAsTransparency = !(flags>>3 & 1)`; always together with `alpha_aux = 1` and mostly RGBA PNG: 536+84 of 685 `alpha_aux = 1` PNGs; glow/spec masks); `flags & 2` ⇒ env-map file name follows; bit 0 and 2 are not used by the ctor. Not used here: the `texture_file` names (`body_athroxmale_default.png`) – the rdb ids in the part table are authoritative (`ents[i].name == materials[i].name` for all 778 records). The CAT render (`FUN_10056ed6`) draws with `RViewPort_t::SetMaterial` like scenery, so only `opacity`, `emissive`, `specular × shin_str` and `shin` reach D3D (3727 of 3738 materials have a non-white diffuse, 3730 an `ambient != diffuse`, ambient mostly 0.9: none of it is used; 90 materials have a non-zero emissive; 1027 have SPECULARENABLE by the `UpdateSpecular` rule).
- **Textures**: `parts[i].texture` = `rdb_1010004` id (JPEG or PNG; 3738 parts: 9 without id, 8 ids not in the database). Seven 1010004 records (shared by 178 character materials) start with `02 00 00 00 0D 0A 1A 0A` instead of the PNG signature; the remainder is a valid PNG, `texture::decode_texture` repairs them. `hands_*_default.png` (e.g. 9235) is a solid green (0,255,0) 128×64 placeholder, as is Atrox's `head_athroxmale_default.png` (9232, on a 3-vertex/1-triangle `head` part): green (`RGB565 0x07e0`) is the client's **chroma key** for cloth textures, see **Skin**. Player bodies take their textures from rdb **1010011** (naked skin) and the worn cloth, not from the model's own `*_default.png`; see **Skin**. (GUI.dll's `COMMAND_DEBUG_SKIN_{HANDS,BODY,FEET,ARMS,LEGS}` GM key bindings name the same five slots.) `VisualCATMesh_t::s_nTextureType` [DisplaySystem 0x100af87c] = 1010004 by default; `FUN_100726f6` [0x100726f6] switches it to 1010016/1010017 by a DValue (texture quality), unused here.

### Animation record (1010003)
```
char[32] root; u32 n_event; n_event × { u32 time_ms; char[32] name }        markers (loopstart, left, right, attack, …)
u32 3 (magic); u32 version | 0x1000000 (compressed; all 3190 records: 0x105 ×2667, 0x106 ×523)
u32/f32 duration_ms (u32 if version 0x105, f32 if ≥ 0x106); u32 signature; f32 param; u32 n_track
i8 rot_bits, i8 trans_bits
five delta streams, in this order and in this shape:  (bits, columns)
   idx   (32,1)  per track: bone, unused, mode, n_rot_keys, n_trans_keys   (the last is read after the rotation keys)
   time  (20 if 0x105 else 32, 1)  per key: time (float bit pattern if 0x106, integer ms if 0x105); rot keys then trans keys of a track
   rot   (rot_bits, 4)             per rot key 4 values: q = (v − 2^(rot_bits−1)), then normalised, order x y z w
   range (32, 1)                   per track: f32 bit patterns x0 x1 y0 y1 z0 z1 (translation box)
   trans (trans_bits, 3)           per trans key 3 values: p_i = (hi_i − lo_i) · v / (2^trans_bits − 1) + lo_i
delta stream = per column { u32 comp_len; u32 raw_len; zlib blob }, raw = big-endian ceil(bits/8)-byte words;
   every column is a running sum (wrapping u32) masked to `bits`; successive reads cycle through the columns, the word offset advances after the last column
```
(`FUN_1005bb82`: ctor(bits, io, columns); `FUN_1005b96a`: read.) All 3190 records decode, consuming each record up to ≤12 trailing bytes; `rot`/`trans` key counts per track are independent. The uncompressed branch (flag clear: `t, q[4]` then `t, p[3]` per key) is never used in the data and not decoded. `mode` is the authored per-bone layer bitmask (often 2), not a controller mode; `param` ~0 for most clips and ≈0.5 for a few (unknown). Time unit is milliseconds, confirmed by the clock trace below.
- **Sampling** (`FUN_10051d2a/df4`): binary search for the surrounding keys; translation lerp, rotation shortest-path slerp (lerp when 1−|dot| < ε); before the first/after the last key the end key is held. `ActorRig::pose/head_attractor` preserve the supplied time, including exactly `duration`; playback callers control looping. Applying modulo in the rig wrapped terminal death/corpse poses to the first frame.
- **Clock (RE)**: DisplaySystem `VisualCATMesh_t::RunFunction` 0x10074bb4 passes scaled frame seconds to `FUN_10074228`, then `FUN_10072fde` advances by `abs(dt * 1000 * speedScale)` (`_DAT_1008aa78` is the double 1000.0). Randy `CATKeyframeAnim_t::SetTime` 0x10051cfc stores that time unchanged at +0x50. Milliseconds are therefore confirmed, not merely inferred; `param` is not used in this delta expression.
- **Unresolved `param`**: Randy reader 0x10052068 stores it at `CATKeyframeAnimData_t+0x30`. DisplaySystem `AnimationManager::Get` 0x1006f13a extracts duration at data +0x2c and marker metadata, not this float; the traced clock chain above does not consume it. Its meaning remains unresolved, so it is preserved without inventing a timing multiplier.
- **Authored layers and composition (fresh RE)**: Randy `CATKeyframeAnim_t::SetLayers` 0x10051f51 calls 0x10051f19: a bone is enabled iff `playback_layers & data->mode[bone] != 0`. DisplaySystem 0x100724a1 constructs every clip with playback mask 3. `FUN_10074180` traverses animation nodes in priority groups, accumulates preceding nodes' `Layer_e` values, and 0x100729f9 applies `~(accumulated & 3) & 3` to lower-priority clips. Thus `Layer_e` 1 is an exclusion bit for lower-priority clips, not an instruction to sample the action itself with mask 2; no bone-name upper-body mask exists in this path.
- **Blend chain**: DisplaySystem 0x10074228 sets each node's `CATAnimBlend_t::anim1` to the preceding chain, with its own clip as `anim2`; a fully blended node whose effective mask is greater than 2 discards the preceding chain. Randy 0x10050742 builds per-bone presence bits (1 previous, 2 current). For presence 3, 0x10050586 slerps rotation and 0x1005063c lerps translation by the node weight; presence 1 or 2 passes the sole enabled source through regardless of weight. Presence 0 falls through to the previous source when present, otherwise the current source; absent tracks retain identity/zero. `ActorRig::*_layered` accepts a borrowed ordered slice of `anim::AnimLayer { clip, ms, layers, blend }`, shares the sampler for skin/mounts/head/effect anchors, and adds no layer-list allocation. The original single-clip methods remain equivalent mask-3 wrappers.
- **Dispatch is separate from lookup**: a name-table id's generic layer does not override an explicit layer supplied by its original action producer. In particular the wield name-table entry is overlay, while the traced wield gesture dispatch explicitly supplies body layer 0 (Gamecode chain documented in `zone/combat-anim.md` §4). Avatar/Dynels must preserve independent base/action clocks, pass the original producer's layer, use the same ordered chain for all anchors, and retain authored markers on the action clock. Regression: `character::anim::tests::authored_layers_blend_and_pass_through_sparse_tracks` (not run during this change).
- **Playback priority and fades (fresh GC + DS RE)**: Gamecode 0x1003bc11 stores signed priority at playback-entry +0x19, separate from `Layer_e` at +0x18; 0x1003bd7a → 0x100108be forwards both to `SetAnimation`. Movement 0x1006be27 uses priority 0 / infinite count; generic CharacterAction 0x64 and NewLevel use 0x10010d83: priority −2 / count 1 / name-table layer; use/equipment 0x10081e74 supplies priority −1 / count 1 / explicit body layer 0. Nodes compose priority-descending, chronological within each priority. Therefore a generic overlay at −2 does **not** mean `[action(mask 3), base(mask 2)]`: with base priority 0 / exclusion 0 its effective mask is 3; only preceding higher-priority exclusion bits restrict it.
- **Exact positive-count fade**: DS 0x10073f23 derives remaining time of the preceding same-priority clip (`duration − fmod(elapsed,duration)`) and clamps to 200–300 ms; default 200 ms without a preceding clip. Float words are 0x100af8a0 = `0x43480000` (200), 0x100af89c = `0x43960000` (300). DS 0x10072562 begins `clamp(ms/fade,0,1)`; only after `ms > fade` switches to `clamp((duration−ms)/fade,0,1)`. Short clips finishing before this switch never fade out. Positive-count predecessor nodes stay until their own duration ends; 0x10074228 only prunes older same-priority nodes when an incoming **negative-count** node reaches full weight. Shared `animation_fade_ms`, `animation_blend`, `animation_layers` implement this arithmetic and priority-group exclusion. `ActorRig::*_composed` accepts a cloneable iterator to sample variable histories without allocating a layer list per frame; slice `*_layered` and single-clip methods remain available.
- **Remote holder nodes**: `Dynels::play_action(character, id, original_layer, priority)` and `play_swing` share positive-count node histories, independent clip/variant/rate/marker clocks, and the same priority/mask/fade iterator for rendering and effect anchors. A swing node stores its list key and slot: every live matching key suppresses a duplicate, while a different key adds a node even for an identical clip. A later normal swing, group-0 FightStop idle, or priority-−3 death must not cancel older priority-−1 swing nodes. `FiredNote { id, slot }` routes each marker through the original slot's retained damage/flags and the current target; slot −1 action notes cannot consume weapon context. Gamecode 0x1003c036 flushes unfired attack-related notes (`attack`, `attack_effect_1..4`) only when a holder node loses its CAT handle, not when another swing starts. Remote nodes retain their terminal frame and flush that subset when their own duration expires. Regressions: `retail_priority_masks_and_positive_count_fades`, `authored_layers_blend_and_pass_through_sparse_tracks`, `distinct_swing_lists_preserve_parallel_holder_nodes` (includes Burst → normal at 50 ms and Fling → FightStop at 133 ms), `interrupted_holder_flushes_only_pending_attack_markers`, and layered rifle/head/mount equivalence in `real_rifle_effect_anchor_uses_authored_muzzle_and_animated_hand` (not run during this change).
- Totals: 3190 clips, 6 648 373 keys, 7520 s of animation; 168 skeleton hashes in clips vs 167 in models: 8 clips fit no model, 2 models have no clip.

### Scene assembly (`character::load_character*`)
- `load_character(store, id)` = bind pose; `load_character_posed(store, id, anim_id, time_s)` = CPU skinning of the same vertices at `time_s` (looped) with the clip's bone matrices, error if the clip's `signature` ≠ the model's. `load_character_record(store, rdb_type, id, pose)` accepts 1010027 too.
- Output follows the scene contract: Z negated, winding reversed (exactly as for static meshes), UVs untouched; one `Submesh` per material (groups merged); blend: opacity < 1 or fractional alpha ⇒ `AlphaBlend`, only 0/255 alpha ⇒ `AlphaTest`, both only if `flags & 8 == 0`; every submesh carries white × opacity (the D3D diffuse / ambient of a CAT draw is white, see *Vertex lighting*; the earlier untextured-diffuse tint is gone), `emissive` = the material's emissive, specular by the `UpdateSpecular` rule. `spawn`/`spawn_look_at` frame the model from the front (characters face +Z in model space, −Z in the scene).
- **Heads**: `load_character_with_head(store, id, head_mesh_id, pose)` mounts a static `1010001` head mesh on the model's `Attractor01_head` (bone `Bip01 Head`, `pos`/`rot` in that bone's frame): `mount = W[bone] ∘ (R(rot), pos)`, applied as the instance transform (conjugated by the Z mirror). Player bodies have no skin vertices on the head bone, so in the bind pose its frame is derived: nearest fitted ancestor frame carried down the chain with the local transforms of the compatible clip whose first frame is closest to the fitted bind frames (`best_rest_clip`). Gamecode builds the head file names `head_<race><sex>%02d{,_lores,_hires}.abiff` (strings in GUI.dll); the file name → rdb id lookup is the name table (below). When a head is mounted the body's own `head` stub part is dropped.
- **SmoothAnimations (live)**: DisplaySystem `VisualCATMesh_t::RunFunction` `10074bb4` reads EnvPrefs byte14 (registered `1005fd60`, bool callback `1005fd4f`). Enabled sets `RCATMesh+21c=0`; disabled selects 0/1/3/6 from camera-to-feet distance squared divided by the unscaled resource sphere radius squared, at thresholds 256/1024/2304 (`10090b18/14/10`). This is frame refresh decimation, not a change to clip time, key interpolation, notes, or blending. Randy `1005798b` decrements `+218` only when the previous raster frame was visible, resetting a negative counter to `+21c`; `10055d52` samples bones only with counter below1. Both viewer paths feed previous body visibility back to own/remote CAT pose scheduling; retained shared posed geometry also serves picking/effect sources, and unchanged Arc poses are not uploaded again. Resource radius follows `CATMesh_t::CalcBoundingVolume` `10052f16` → `BVolume_t::Init` `10029e51` → `10029b7e`: ordered Ritter expansion over every authored bind vertex. Native extrema `10029a70` compare X for the second minimum and Y for the third; those quirks are preserved rather than replaced by an invented AABB sphere. Regressions: `native_distance_bands_and_visibility_countdown`, `native_resource_sphere_expands_in_authored_order`, installed-data `smooth_animation_toggle_changes_native_refresh_not_clip_time`; not executed by this worker.
- **Native GPU skinning regression contract**: `Scene::native_skin` retains the CAT local positions, bone indices, weight and bone-0 normal; `ActorFrame::skin_pose` shares the sampled world-bone palette. This moves only the arithmetic of Randy `FUN_100543bc` / `FUN_1005470d` to compute, preserving the `FUN_100540a5` CPU bone recursion, the `weight >= 0.999` single-bone branch, scalar rotation/translation order, normal length guard (`length > 1e-12`, otherwise +Y), and scene Z mirror. An empty palette restores the authored static/bind vertices; an absent palette retains the last pose. Head/weapon mount transforms and effect anchors remain CPU-composed from the same retained pose. Own/crowd drawing submits palettes and mounts; exact CPU body vertices are generated lazily for picking or source-mesh effects. The picking broadphase is centered at the bind-mesh AABB center (computed once per model), expanded by twice the native resource radius plus `POSE_MARGIN`; exact box/triangle picking computes the actual retained skin bounds.
- **Skin compute precision and CPU precedence**: Metal uses a dedicated raw MSL kernel with `#pragma METAL fp math_mode(safe)`, `#pragma METAL fp contract(off)` and `precise::sqrt` / `precise::divide`; other rendering shaders are unchanged, and non-Metal uses WGSL. The [Metal Shading Language specification](https://developer.apple.com/metal/Metal-Shading-Language-Specification.pdf) §1.6.3 documents the precision/contraction pragmas and §6.6 the precise math namespace. This is an implementation strategy for matching existing CPU arithmetic, not evidence of a passing byte/pixel gate. If a frame supplies both an explicit CPU `skin` and a palette, the CPU vertices win: source-mesh effects may intentionally deform or recolour them. The byte regression submits divergent position/colour lanes with both fields to enforce this precedence.
- **Headless regression proof (Metal, 2026-10-16)**: `cargo test --release -j 2 -p ao-render native_skin_ -- --nocapture` exercises the production compute dispatch and maps its actual actor vertex buffers back to the CPU. The isolated `native_skin_tests.rs` compares all vertex bytes against scalar CPU arithmetic at the single-bone threshold (including the adjacent lower f32 and an invalid unused bone-1 index), degenerate/near-zero normals, unchanged UV/colour lanes, animated→static→animated transitions and retained poses. With the installed client it additionally compares the existing `ActorRig::pose_composed_cached` oracle against all 60 actors' GPU buffers at bind/0/173/611 ms using bodies 5900/5907/5914/5921/5927/5934/5941 and creature 29902, real head meshes and attachments 15839/7796/26163. It renders both CPU and compute paths with native mesh-effect modes, fading and rigid mounts, then requires identical RGBA bytes through the existing `texture_to_rgba` helper (no pixel tolerance); byte diagnostics are reported separately so a float mismatch cannot silently be relabelled pixel equivalence. Missing client assets produce an explicit SKIP; no assets are copied or fixture images generated. All six production Metal regressions passed in the private integration worktree with installed assets. Non-Metal byte/pixel identity has not been verified. Zone-reset coverage also extends the existing churn regression: model-specific vertex-buffer pools are released when their models are cleared, since new model uploads invalidate those pools.

### Name table, animation roles, player characters
- **Name table** = `rdb_1000010` id 1 (1.4 MB, `character::NameTable`): `u32 version = 7`, then sections `{ u32 rdb_type; u32 n; n × { u32 id; u32 len; char name[len] } }` for 1010001 (11828 meshes, `*.abiff`), 1010002 (805 models, `*.cir`: `athrox_male.cir` = 5900, `solitus_male.cir` = 5907, …), 1010003 (3559 clips, `*.ani`), 1010004 (13177 textures), 1010005 (empty), 1010008 (11040 icons/textures), 1010011 (60 skins). The sibling singletons 1000045/1000032 are unrelated event/data tables. char_stats: names for 767/770 models and 3168/3190 clips.
- **Clip sets / roles**: the client names clips `%s_%s_01_01.ani` (set, clip name; Gamecode.dll string). All seven player bodies share one skeleton; the set is `athrox` for Atrox, `male`/`female` for the other breeds (so opifex/nanomage use the solitus sets). Clip names present in all three sets include `idle-stand`, `idle-unarmed`, `walk`, `walk-back|left|right`, `run`, `run-back`, `sneakcool`, `idle-chair`, `idle-ground`, `idle-crawl`, `swim`, `idle-swim`, `jump-stand`, `jump-forward`, `idle-sleep-ground`, `idle-lounging`, `idle-hover`, plus attack/die/weapon clips and ~90 `social-*` emotes (`social-backflip`, `social-bow`, …); there is no `run-left/right`, `fall`, `wave`, `laugh-*`, `sleep` clip (those strings in Gamecode are other tables). `character::{model_clips, character_animations, role_anim}`; `Role` = the named roles above + `Emote(name)`, `FromStr` accepts the clip name (`walk`, `idle`, `social-bow`). Walk ids: athrox 10078, male 10191, female 10162; run 9386/10194/10146; idle-stand 9992/10173/10135. Which clip the game logic picks when (movement state machine) is code, not data: the table gives the name only.
- **Heads**: `head_<race><sex>[_asian|_african]<NN>.abiff` with race `athrox` (no sex: Atrox is male only in the data), `nano`, `opifex`, `solitus`; counts: atrox 41, nano m/f 41/43, opifex m/f 46/50, solitus m/f 57/61 (+ asian 9/7, african 9/8); `NN` is sparse (0..74). No `_lores/_hires` meshes exist in the database. Also named one-off heads (`head_solitusmale_joshua_kahn`, mutants, …). `player_heads(store, breed, gender, skin)` → `(NN, mesh id)`.
- **Skin (DisplaySystem.dll / Gamecode.dll / GUI.dll; corrects the earlier "unequipped = naked" reading)**. A player body's textures are *layers* per material, set with `VisualCATMesh_t::SetCATTexture(material name | index, rdb id, TextureLayer, AlphaMode)` [DisplaySystem 0x100729b4 / 0x100729d6 → `FUN_100700e9` @0x100700e9]. Layer 0 = skin (rdb **1010011**; `FUN_1006fe72` @0x1006fe72 picks type 1010011 for layer 0, `s_nTextureType` = 1010004 otherwise), 1 = the material's base texture (1010004), 2 = worn cloth (1010004), 3 = env/second texture (`RMaterial_t::SetEnvTexture`, `FUN_10073e4f` @0x10073e4f). `FUN_10073e4f` stores layers 0/1/2 in a per-material record (layer 0 → +0xc `FUN_1007279d`, 1 → +8 `FUN_10072763`, 2 → +0x10 `FUN_100727e4`). The material's final texture is `FUN_1007457f` @0x1007457f (from `FUN_10074b03`, into `RCATMesh_t::CreateSubstMaterial`): `overlay = layer2 ?? layer1`, `skin = layer0`; **both → `FUN_10074393` @0x10074393**, the RGB565 composite: the skin stretched to the overlay's size, every overlay texel replaces it **except `0x07e0` = pure green (the chroma key)**; an overlay without green texels is returned as is. `SetSkinData(breed, sex, race)` [`VisualCATMesh_t::SetSkinData` 0x1007298b → `FUN_10070439` @0x10070439] loops `ClothPart` 0..4 = `hands body feet arms legs` (table behind `ClothData_t::GetName` @GameData 0x1000a5f0), builds `FUN_1006ff7f` @0x1006ff7f = `<part>_<breed><sex>[_<race>]_naked.png` (breed `solitus/opifex/nanomage/athrox`; sex forced to `male` for Atrox; race `caucation/african/asian` only for Solitus, `BreedRace_e` 1/2/3) and sets it on layer 0 of the material of that name. Callers: `CCCharacter_t::ChangeMesh` [GUI 0x1011ab53] / `ChangeHead` [0x1011a631] (creation), `CharacterViewer_c::Update` [GUI 0x100054cd] (select), the world (`FUN_10058078` @Gamecode 0x10058078, only for a dynel with a head mesh = a player). Worn cloth: `ClothData_t {part, tex, tex2}` → `SetCATTexture(ClothData_t::GetName(part), tex, 2)` (+ layer 3 for `tex2`), Gamecode `FUN_1004b5ab` @0x1004b5ab / `FUN_1007e7e2` / GUI `Update`; per slot `FUN_1004b521` @0x1004b521 takes the highest non-empty of three tiers; an empty slot gets layer 2 = 0 (`FUN_10046992` @0x10046992). **What an unequipped player shows: the model's own `<part>_<race><sex>_default.png` composited over the skin with green keyed out** — armour-looking body/legs/arms/feet (olive/brown Solitus suit, white top + orange trousers Opifex, black suits Nanomage, orange vest Atrox), skin-coloured hands and face because `hands_*_default.png` and Atrox's head stub are solid green. This is what the retail creation screen shows (user screenshot of \"SELECT BREED\", compared with `aomac view player` renders of all 7 combos). `[INFERENCE, unresolved]` the static trace could not find the writer of the layer-1 slot (records are created with a null base, `FUN_1007269e`; layer 1 is only reached through `FUN_10073e4f`; `FUN_10074b03` swaps in `RTexture_t::GetDefault` (a 16×16 placeholder) when the result equals the bare skin), so the default-under-cloth rule rests on the screenshot and on the green-keyed data, not on a located call. The 8→5/6-bit rounding of `RTexture_t::Load` is also unresolved (`overlay_on_skin` truncates; only near-green texels differ). Layer 3 (env texture of an item) is not in `Equipment` and not rendered. The three `body_{solitus,opifex,nanomage}female_clad.png` 1010011 skins (55738–55740) are referenced by no client code. The Miiir underwear items (`body_underwear-female_model1..8`, `legs_mens-underwear1..7`, item records e.g. 152907/154047) are ordinary green-keyed cloth textures.
- **Appearance cache `prefs/CharacterViewer.xml`** (`CharacterViewerModule_c` [GUI]; `character::ViewerCache`, `CachedCharacter`, `ClothEntry`, `MeshEntry`). Path = `AnarchyPath_t::GetPrefsPath()` + `CharacterViewer.xml` (ctor 0x10006ac9; the client dir's `prefs/`, global, not per character). Lifecycle: the constructor runs `LoadCache` [0x100065a4]; `SlotShuttingDown` [0x10006aad] and `SlotConfigurationSaved` [0x10006ac4] run `UpdateCache` [0x100069ed] then `SaveCache` [0x100060c0] (also the destructor [0x100063b1]). `UpdateCache` reads the `CharacterID` DValue and stores `GetData(id)` of the live world (`N3Msg_GetCharacterVisuals`; unless the character is in a mech or morphed) into a `std::map<id, CharacterViewerData_t>`: `Time` = `_time64`, `MeshID` = stat 0xc, `HeadID` = 0x40, `Breed` = 4, `Sex` = 0x3b, `Fatness` = 0x2f, `Side` = 0x21, the visible cloth list (`N3Msg_GetCharacterVisibleClothes` → `FUN_1004b521`: one entry per `ClothPart`, texture 0 for an empty slot) and the attractor meshes. `GetData(id, data, useCache)` without a live world falls back to the map. `LoadCache` merges only characters with all eight `Int32` (`ID Time MeshID HeadID Breed Sex Fatness Side`), skipping incomplete `Cloth` (`AlphaMode BodyPart EnvTextureId Priority TextureID`, all `Int32`) and `Mesh` (`Attractor Flags` `Int8`, `MeshID TextureID` `Int32`) children; an existing id is replaced unless it has a larger `Time`. Format = `Message::SaveToFile/LoadFromFile` [Utils.dll 0x1000a8a4 / 0x10009fd5] via `DumpToXML`/`LoadFromXML` [0x1000a0e4 / 0x10009f10] (TinyXML): root `<Archive code="0">`, typed value elements `<Int32 name="ID" value="42" />`, a nested message `<Archive code="0" name="Cloth">`, a name with several values wrapped in `<Array name="Cloth">` whose children have no `name`; no XML declaration, 4-space indent; the map is written in ascending id order. `CharacterViewer_c::Update` applies a hit: body model = `MeshID` (so thin/fat/robe bodies come from the cache), `SetSkinData(breed, sex, race of HeadID's head-table entry)`, the `Mesh` entries via `AddAttractorMesh` (the head is the attractor-0 entry), and every `Cloth` entry via `SetCATTexture(part, TextureID, 2)` (+ `EnvTextureId` on layer 3 when non-zero); `Fatness`/`Side`/`Priority`/`AlphaMode` are not read by `Update`. A fresh install has no file (then `SetBreed`). aomac: `ViewerCache::load(dir)` / `parse` / `update(CachedCharacter)` / `save(dir)` / `to_xml`, `CachedCharacter::{equipment, set_equipment, head_mesh}`, `load_cached_character`; the select preview (`play/preview.rs`) reads `~/Library/Application Support/aomac/CharacterViewer.xml`, then `<client>/prefs/CharacterViewer.xml` (read-only), for the selected `CharacterData.id`. M3: when the worn cloth arrives, build `Equipment` → `CachedCharacter::set_equipment`, `ViewerCache::load(prefs::dir())`, `.update(c)`, `.save(dir)`. Not done: layer-3 env textures.
- **Cached preview mounts**: `load_cached_character` routes every positive non-head cached `Mesh` through `ActorRig::new`, then uses `ActorRig::pose` for the body vertices and per-mesh scene instances. This shares the world renderer's rigid mounting rule (`Attractor<place+1>_*`, bone world frame composed with attractor position/rotation and conjugated by the Z mirror) for weapons and armour, including non-hand places; unknown places are skipped by the rig. The attractor-0 head still overrides `HeadID`, worn cloth still uses the skin composite, and the cache entries/flags are not modified. `CharacterViewer_c::Update` [GUI 0x100054cd] applies cached meshes with `AddAttractorMesh`; captured rifle 15839 uses place 1 / flags 2, heads flags 4 (`zone_wear_rifle_borealis.rec`, `zone/avatar.md` §7). The meanings of other mesh flags and `TextureID` remain unresolved, as in the world attachment path; no guessed flag filtering is applied. `cached_meshes_follow_the_rigs_head_hands_and_other_attractors` compares bind/animated preview instances with the existing rig for a head override, both hands and non-hand place 5 (captured light 26163, `zone/dynel.md` §1.3).
- **API**: `Player::new(breed, gender, skin, head)` / `Player { …, equipment: Equipment }` with `Equipment([Option<u32>; 5])` = rdb 1010004 cloth texture per `ClothPart` (`Equipment::wear(part, tex)`; `None` = nothing worn = the model's own texture over the skin), `load_player(store, &player, Some((Role, secs)))`, `load_player_build(store, &player, build, pose)` (`build` 0/1/2 = `_thin`/normal/`_fat`, `CCCharacter_t::ChangeMesh`), `load_player_character(store, breed, gender, head_nn, pose)` = unequipped caucasian shortcut; `player_model`, `skin_texture_name`, `overlay_on_skin`. The composite of a slot has key `{ rdb_type: 0x4000_0000 | skin id, id: overlay texture id }`. CLI: `aomac view player <breed> <gender> [--head NN --skin asian --role walk --time 0.6]`, `… --cache <dir> --char-id <id>` (renders that `CharacterViewer.xml` entry), `aomac view char <id> --role walk`.
- **NPC body key regression**: `actor::npc_part_textures` applies the same `overlay_on_skin` rule to humanoid default and wire diffuse textures, with worn cloth replacing (not layering over) the default. The skin source is the NPC record's `HeadMesh` (`FUN_100c2c15`, NPC documentation §4), not the mounted head list. `humanoid_defaults_and_cloth_key_to_real_skin` surveys all seven breed/sex bodies, three builds and skin choices and compares each source `RGB565 0x07e0` texel to its expected scaled skin texel for player/default/NPC wire/cloth paths. Integrated verification reported by CharVisuals: 35/35 character tests passed, including keyed-path and humanoid NPC-record surveys. Live ICC offscreen frame inspected by CharVisuals: Brandon Thorn's two hands are skin-coloured in his teal suit, replacing the user's reported pure-green hand. No head/hair or arbitrary-green filtering; the player's legitimate green hair remains unchanged.

### Results
`char_stats` over all records: 1010002 770/770 decoded (3 757 273 vertices, 2 948 954 triangles, 3738 texture parts, 0 failures, every record builds a bind-pose scene), 1010027 8/8, 1010003 3190/3190; posed decode of all 768 models that have a compatible clip with its first clip at mid-time: 768/768 ok. Tests: `character::tests` + `anim::tests` (hand-built records incl. zlib delta streams, skinning, blend rules), `tests/character_real.rs` (all models/clips parse, upright + 99 % normal agreement, posing/looping/skeleton check, head mounts, skips without the client).
Visual check (`aomac view char …`, screenshots inspected): 5900/5907/5927/5941/5914/5934 with their default heads (40098/40681/40630/40135/40250/40209) upright, un-mirrored, textured, heads seated on the neck; 5900 posed with `athrox_sneakcool` 9382 at 0.3–2.1 s (stride with raised leg and swinging arms, stays human-shaped; originally mislabelled walk); 5914 bind vs idle clip 9375 frame 0 identical proportions (bone scales); creatures 29902, 85335, 95326 and android 15215 posed with their first compatible clip. Round 3: `aomac view player <breed> <gender> --role idle|walk --time 0.6` for solitus/opifex/nanomage × male/female and atrox male (7 combinations; Atrox female does not exist in the client): naked skins, no green on hands/feet, heads seated, idle = relaxed stance, walk at 0.6 s = mid-stride (one leg forward, arms swinging); solitus `--skin asian --head 2` shows the asian head set with a darker skin; skin tones per race: solitus tan/brown, opifex slate-grey, nanomage grey-brown, atrox ochre-brown.
**Live body resolver (resolved).** Gamecode `10058078` → `10057ff7` → `10057eb3` constructs `<breed>_<sex>[_thin|_fat][_robe][_hires|_lores].cir`. Fatness 0/2 alone add thin/fat; Fatness 3 has no suffix. SCFU.mode / AppearanceUpdate.extra → `100572b9` → dynel+0x208 adds robe exactly when 1. Stat **0x378 (888 decimal)** is the optional hires/lores selector, not robe; only its suffix is retried when missing. Native raw strings: `1015f324` thin, `1015f32c` fat, `1015f340` .cir. `native_live_body_names_preserve_robe_and_only_fallback_resolution` covers installed named keys, including absent variants without invented substitutes.

**Gaps.** `env_texture`/env maps, the progressive-mesh LOD trailer and the `param`/`alpha_aux` semantics are decoded but not used; the movement/combat state machine choosing a clip (only the names are data), the mapping from the server's item records to `Equipment` textures (server-side data; the API takes the texture ids), Atrox female (absent from the client), the exact skin tint of the in-game lighting; uncompressed `CATAnim` keys and mesh version 0x67 are not supported (absent from the data); the bind frame of skinless bones is an approximation.

**Room lightmaps** (`dungeon::depack_lightmap`, `record.rs`). The room record stores `i32 size + 4`, `i32 vertex count`, then `size` bytes: a zlib stream (zlib 1.2.5, `FUN_1002a180`) that `n3Room_t::DepackLightmap(count, mono)` (N3 @0x10010eb7) inflates to `count * 2` bytes (`mono` = room format version > 7, which is every record; `count * 6` otherwise). `CreateDungeonRoom` (@0x10008563) applies it with `FUN_100082a7`: u16 *i* becomes the diffuse colour of **final shell vertex *i*** (welded, in emission order) `R = (v & 0x1f) << 3`, `G = (v >> 5 & 0x3f) << 2`, `B = (v >> 8) & 0xf8`, alpha 255 (a stream shorter than `count*2` paints every vertex white). `VisualRoom_t::Init` (DisplaySystem @0x1006e133) sets `D3DRS_DIFFUSEMATERIALSOURCE/SPECULAR/AMBIENT = material` and `EMISSIVEMATERIALSOURCE = COLOR1`, material ambient 0.8, diffuse white: **the baked colour is emissive light**, `lit = tex * (lightmap + 0.8 * ambient + dynamic lights)`. We emit `Submesh.prelit` (renderer: `tex * material * (vertex.rgb + 0.8 * ambient)`, no sun / point lights). Values are dark (mean ≈ (36, 48, 44)/255, 38 % zero) because they are *added* to the ambient term.
To make the vertex order match the client, the weld was ported exactly: the candidates of `FUN_10001645` are the vertices of the cells (x−1..x+1, z−1) (ring `A[0]..A[3]` of per-cell vertex counts in `CreateDungeonRoom`) and of the previous cell up to the current block (`A[w]`), compared by position / normal / uv only (**not** per material), in index order; the piece order inside a cell is corner posts, floor piece, main piece (`FUN_10001c3b` list build). Result over all 2000 rooms of the 601 playfields: vertex count == lightmap count for **1991 (99.55 %)**, see *Lightmap oracle* in *Dungeon rooms*; the 9 rooms that still differ keep the plain ambient look. Indoor ambient = record ambient bytes (max with 0.01) instead of the old 0.45 fill, no sun.
*Material slot 0*: most tilemaps list material id 1 first (unpainted cells use layer value 0 → rdb 1010009/1, the "Error" placeholder image of the PRK database). `VisualRoom_t::AddMaterial` (@0x1006e916) pairs every material with the fallback texture `darkrock_tileable.png`, so material 1 is drawn with that texture.

## audio

Crate `ao-audio` (+ `playfield/zone.rs`). Everything below was reverse engineered from the original DLLs (`SandyInterface.dll` "SI" = sound glue, `SIMPlayer.dll` "SIM" = interactive music, `Gamecode.dll` "GC", `GameData.dll` "GD", `N3.dll`, `serialize.dll`, `mss32.dll` Miles) and decoded from `cd_image/sound`. Labels: **[CODE]** read in the decompile, **[DATA]** decoded from disk, **[INFERENCE]**, **[UNRESOLVED]**.

### Files and decoding (`decode.rs`, example `audio_survey`)
`cd_image/sound`: `sfx/` (4093 wav), `voice/` (43), `music/env/<layer>/*.wav` (1278), `SourceFiles/SM_Sandy_{Game_Dummy,Gui}.sbf` (sound definitions), `music/env/anarchy.sws` (music project), 168 `sfx/player/*.txt` (player FX scripts, unused), `redist/win32` (Miles ASI decoders). Decoding is symphonia (pure Rust) for PCM / IMA / MS ADPCM / Vorbis; **MPEG layer 3 in RIFF (`fmt ` tag 0x55)**, which symphonia's WAV demuxer rejects, is handled by feeding the `data` chunk to its MPEG demuxer (`riff_mp3`). `cargo run --release -p ao-audio --example audio_survey -- [client]` (default `~/Games/ProjectRubiKa/client`; exits 2 with a usage line when `cd_image/sound` is missing): **5244 of 5244 files decode, 0 failures, 6.9 h of audio** — wav PCM 487 (11/22/44.1 kHz, mono+stereo), wav IMA-ADPCM 3155, wav MPEG-L3 1174 (all the music, 44.1 kHz stereo), ogg Vorbis 428; `sfx/env/nosound.wav` is IMA-ADPCM with an empty `data` chunk = valid silence (0 frames). The client decodes the same set (`AIL_file_type`: wav / ASI mp3 type 0xd / ogg type 0x10, `PrefetchSample` @SI 0x1000227e).

### Engine rules [CODE] (`mixer.rs`, `game.rs::attenuation`)
* **No Miles 3D, no rolloff factor, no provider selection, no panning, Doppler only on the five ShipEngine sounds.** Imports of SI: `AIL_open_digital_driver`, `AIL_allocate_sample_handle`, `AIL_set_sample_volume_levels`, `AIL_start_sample`, ...; SIMPlayer adds the `AIL_*_stream` calls (music = AIL streams). Driver: `SE_Initialise` (SI @0x10007c5a) `AIL_set_preference(3, 16)`, `AIL_open_digital_driver(44100, 16 bit, 2 ch)`; **13 SFX sample handles** (`SoundEngineInterface_t+0x14`, ctor @0x10007d0b; music streams are outside the pool and have their own 3 handles (`mixer::MAX_STREAMS`: `SIMPlayer_t+0x30..0x38` = playing / fading old / preloaded next; a fourth `AIL_open_stream` returns 0 → `FUN_10009ed5` 0 → `FUN_1000a143` reports event 0xd, drops the pending transition and keeps the current stream; `Mixer::play` returns 0 for it, `MusicPlayer` keeps the old sample and searches again); `AIL_set_preference(3, 16)` sets Miles' per-driver sample-handle count: `_AIL_set_preference@8` mss32 @0x1001d950 stores into the array at `DAT_1005d100` (defaults `[0]=5 ms period, [1]=16 timers, [2]=1 MMX, [3]=64`), slot 3 is read by the digital driver open `FUN_1002acb0` as `nSamples` (`nSamples × 0x7fc` bytes of SAMPLE structures): 16 = 13 SFX + 3 SIMPlayer streams). `AllocateChannel` (@0x10007d5c, disasm checked): (1) fewer than 13 in use → `AIL_allocate_sample_handle`; (2) else the **oldest** list entry whose sample is not `PLAYING` gives its handle away; (3) else, for `p = 2 down to the request's priority`, the **oldest** entry of priority `p` with a live handle is `AIL_end_sample`d and its handle reused (exactly one victim; `inUse` is neither changed nor leaked: the handle only changes owner); priority > 2 never steals; (4) nothing → NULL, not played, retried next frame while the sound is active. Priority = sbf record byte 6 bits 5–6 (0 highest: 503 env/ambience records; 1 = 5320 default; 2 = 17); only sounds started with `SE_Play2DSound` are on the list, looping ones (`SE_Play2DSoundLooping`) are never stolen. Whole function runs under `LockSIMPlayer`; no crash path in normal use (the steal scan has no null check on list entries, never null; the end-sample loop would spin if Miles never reported DONE). Implemented in `Mixer::play` (`MAX_VOICES = 13`, `VoiceDesc::priority`, oldest-first order, test `voice_pool_priority_stealing`) — the reclaim step is implicit (our finished voices leave the pool at once).
* **Attenuation is software, linear, by distance to the listener *position*** (`SetListenerRotPos` @SI 0x1000740f copies only the position): `SandyInterface_t::PlaySample` @0x10002d98 — only when the position vector is non-zero: `d > maxd` plays nothing; `d > mind`: `vol *= ((maxd−mind) − (d−mind)) / (maxd−mind)`. Caller radius `r` (u16): `maxd = r`, `mind = r` or, if `def.min < def.max`, `mind = def.min / def.max · r`. All named sounds have `min 0, max 15 m` ⇒ a radius-R emitter fades linearly from the emitter to R metres. `AIL_set_sample_volume_levels(h, v, v)`: **both channels get the same level, no pan** (our mixer: mono → both channels at full level). Attenuation is only evaluated when `PlaySample` is called; an emitter called every frame follows the camera, a one-shot keeps its start level. Implemented: `attenuation()` (unit test with the formula's values).
* Volume chain (SI @0x10006d37..): `Total_FX = master·FX·mutes`, `Total_Music = master·Music·mutes`; all statics and the prefs defaults (`cd_image/gui/Default/LoginPrefs.xml`: `MasterVolume 1.0, FXVolume 1.0, MusicVolume 1.0, SoundFXOn, MusicOn, BattlemusicMode 3`) are 1.0. `Audio::set_prefs`.
* Gating (`PlaySample` steps 4–9): `prob < 100` sounds are evaluated at most once per whole second with `rand()%200 < prob`; volume = `lerp(vol_min, vol_max)`; a repeated call of an active sound is not restarted, only its level changes; a sound with duration `d` and fade-out `f` called every frame stays at full level `d` seconds after the last call, then fades linearly to 0 over `f` s (`FrameProcessSound` @0x10003b70, `ExecuteCorePlayList` @0x100025fc).
* **ShipEngine Doppler** (`ExecuteCorePlayList` disasm 0x10002770..0x100028c8, only the 5 `SM_Sandy_ShipEngine_*` records = sbf byte 7 bit 5, only for an already playing sound whose emitter velocity `v_e` is non-zero): `u = listener − emitter`, `cos = dot(v_e/|v_e|, u/|u|)` (`−1` when the listener sits on the emitter), `x = clamp(0.03·|v_e|·cos, −1, 1)` (0.03 @0x1000a328), `s = 0.9·s_prev + 0.1·0.3·x` (0.9 @a310, 0.1 @a318, 0.3 @a320; `s` stored at sound+0x180, starts 0), `base = 100 + trunc(100·s)` (−100.0 @a308), `pitch% = trunc(base · f)` with `f = |v_e|` if `0.8 ≤ |v_e| ≤ 1.0` else `0.8` (decoded literally from the x87 sequence, 0.8 @a300) → `AIL_set_sample_playback_rate(base_rate·pitch%/100)`. `mixer::Doppler` + `Mixer::set_pitch` (test `doppler_formula_and_pitch_rate`).

### Sound definitions `.sbf` (`sbf.rs`)
`u32 version = 0x0b; repeat { u32 count; count × record(0xe4 B) [if flags & 0x10: u32 len + len bytes file path, each byte rotl3] [(rec[7] & 0x1f) × u32 child sound ids] }` (parses exactly to EOF; `SetupGameSounds` @SI 0x10006a0a, `ParseSourceFile` @0x10003520). Record fields: flags byte 0 (b1 prefetch/loop, b2 play all children, b3 sequential, b4 has file, b6 random child), play probability (byte 1 bits 0–6), min/max volume (bytes 2/3, /255; all game sounds 127/255 = 0.498), children count (byte 7), child interval min/max and fade-out (u16 at 8/10/12, /100 s), sound id at 0x10 (hash), 46 material-variant ids (0x14), duration max/min (0xcc/0xd0), min dist, max dist, noise radius, fade-in (f32 at 0xd4..0xe0). **Sound id** = `CreateSoundID(name)` (SI @0x10005ad2): case-insensitive rotating xor hash over 4-char big-endian groups (`h ^= rotl(v, i)`); reproduced for 93/93 candidate names. File = `<cd_image>/sound/<file>` as stored (forward slashes, extension as stored, case-insensitive lookup). 6810 game + 85 GUI records; 547 resolve to a name (`SM_Sandy_CC_*`, `SM_Sandy_Env_*`, ...).
* Children: `random_child` = one child per trigger, never the same twice; `play_all` = all at once; `sequential` = round robin; with none of the flags the children are independent timed one-shots (each re-armed with a random value in its own interval while the parent plays, at the parent's level, non-positional; e.g. `BackgroundDay_1` far birds every 15–32 s). Children without a file are silent.

* **`PlayGameSound` arguments and material variants** [CODE, SI `PlayGameSound` 0x10007673, `PlaySample` 0x10002d98, `MapGameMaterialToSoundMaterial` 0x10007490]. `PlayGameSound(id, pos, duration, volume, material, delay, probability,
  size, radius, velocity)` (Gamecode wrapper `FUN_1001117d`; doors / weapon swings pass `(id, pos, 0, 1.0, 0, 0, 100, 1)`, the fight impacts `(id, pos, 0, 1.0, material, 0.4, 100, size)`). `delay` (> 0) queues the call:
  `Frameprocess` counts the entry's timer down and calls `PlaySample` again when it runs out (the position and level are those of the call). The game material (`FabricType` 1..=17; 7 = a player's flesh) goes through
  the jump table `sound_material`: 1..=11 stay, 12 -> 15, 13 -> 7, 14 -> 5, 15 -> 12, 16 -> 13, 17 -> 14, 0 and everything else -> 3. A definition whose 46 variant ids (`0x14`) are not all 0 (`def+0x264`) starts, **besides
  itself**, the variant sound: with `m` = the mapped material, `size` 0 / 2 prefer `variants[m + 30]` / `variants[m + 15]` when that slot is filled (size 1 = plain), then `variants[m]` (else `variants[0]`) for `m` in 1..=45,
  played at the definition's level with no position (`game::variant_of`, `Runtime::play_at(.., material, size)`; so even a door sound with variants plays its slot 3). The impact definitions (`0x113cfb49`, `0x7199b60d`, ... no file, no
  children) are nothing but variant tables: material 7 (flesh) selects the flesh hit sample.

### Music selection [CODE+DATA] (`district.rs`, `game.rs`, `Audio::update`)
1. Gamecode `FUN_100b6d67` runs once per second: zone of the control dynel (`n3Playfield_t::GetZoneInstance`) → `PlayfieldDistrictInfo_t::GetDistrictData(zone)` (GD @0x10009be2: `districts[zone_to_district[zone]]`).
2. `idx = DayPeriod + 1` (**dawn 1, day 2, dusk 3, night 4**; weather overrides: storm 7, rain 6, fog 5 when the weather slot > 0.4, double @GC 0x10167410). `UpdateDayPeriod` (GC @0x1000b1c2) with `GameTime_t` defaults (ctor @0x1000af71): **27 hours of 3600 s per day, dawn 7–9 h, dusk 21–23 h**: `h<7` night, `<9` dawn, `<21` day, `<23` dusk, else night. Our viewer clock (0..6480 s, `GameDayTime`) is that day at **240 s per game hour** (matches the fitted sunrise ≈ 1750 s).
3. `layer = district.music_ids[idx]` (0xffff/−1 = silence); `PlayfieldAnarchy_t::GetDistrictMusicOverride` (GC @0x1012294c, script driven `SetDistrictMusicOverride`) replaces it by `FindLayerID(name)`. **The layer id is the file order of the layer names in `anarchy.sws`** (0..90; there is no name table). Land-control districts (module flag +0xbc, server driven, `district.lc_min != 0`) use `Landcontrol_neutral` (`Audio::set_land_control`; the flag's source is server data, default off). `PlayMusic(layer)` (SI @0x100062e4): same layer = no-op, 0xffff = `Stop(false, true)`, else `SIMPlayer::SignalEvent(layer, force, 2000)` with `force = this+0x10d` = **0** (only the debug `ToggleForceTransitions` sets it). See *Layer change* below. Startup/login music: `PlayStartupMusic` (SI @0x10006554) = layer `mountain\night` (`Audio::play_startup_music`).
4. `[0]` of the 9 stored ids is the **district ambience sound id** (see below); slot 8 is never read.

District table = rdb **1000014**, record id = playfield id (600 records, 4241 districts): `u16 ver (5|6|7) · u32 nzones · u8 ndistricts · districts · nzones × u8 district index`; district (GD `operator>>` @0x100049be, v ≥ 6): `f32 centre[3]; u16 len + name; u16 music_ids[9]; npc levels u16 min,max; land-control levels u16 min,max; u8 respawn %, i32 respawn time, u8 fight mode, u8 nHashInfo; u8 nWildlife, u8 nSpawnPts, u8 nHashSpawnPts; lists`. The sequential reader consumes hash-info `(i32 hash,u16 count)`, wildlife `(u16 catalog index,u16 count)`, spawn points (`f32 xyz,radius`, GD 0x1000a464), then hash spawn points (GD 0x1000640f; rotation reader 0x1000a29a, optional extra points/spell lists/tags). Version 5 has a 32-byte name, four legacy bytes before byte-sized NPC levels, no land-control levels, and one additional legacy hash-list count before the three spawn counts. SQLite's exported records retain 12 zero bytes **after** the zone mapping; taking the final `nzones` bytes incorrectly shifts that mapping. Native 0x10009def reads the mapping immediately after the district list. Real results: **566 Newland City** = ambience id 37, `desert\Day` (dawn/day/dusk) / `desert\Night` (night); 505 Avalon oasis `forest\Gloomy`; 705 `omni\day`/`omni\night`; 127 Condemned Subway `dungeon\cave` (other districts `tribal\night`, `trade\night`); 120 Camelot NO FIGHT `clan\Day`; 3000 `inn\jaz\2/1`; 4688 `EP_01\GardenRedeemed`; The Grid 152, 556, 4001, 4524 and 1948 of the 4241 districts: no music. Tests: `district_music_follows_day_period` and `district::tests::real_districts` (all 600 records, plus Newland's authored wildlife pairs).

Vehicle perk flag **0x100** [CODE]: GC **0x100797b4..0x100797df** bypasses the district restriction in battlestations; otherwise **0x101295f3** obtains the own character's playfield, global vehicle position, `n3Playfield_t::GetZoneInstance`, then `PlayfieldDistrictInfo_t::GetDistrictData`. Activation requires the returned district and nonzero district **+0x48 / +0x44** (land-control maximum/minimum levels). Despite `Feedback_OnlyInOwnLCArea`, this is **not organization/tower ownership**, a bitmap hit, or a radius query; no ownership wire message participates. `FightLevels::own_lca` reuses the authored zone locator and rdb 1000014 district mapping; `Player` publishes `Zone.own_lca` each frame. Missing authored query data remains unavailable (`None`), not an invented accept/refusal. Regression: `fightmode::tests::levels_from_the_district_records_and_updates` compares the gate against the mapped authored LC levels.

Wildlife catalog [CODE+DATA]: Gamecode `Life_c` reader **0x1011ac84**, resource **1010025** (`0xf6969`, not 1001065), shipped record **36258**, SQLite version 1000000, payload 313 bytes. The native stream first reads three DB header i32 words (omitted by the SQLite conversion), then `u16 count`, and each entry is `NUL-terminated name; u16 kind; u32 mesh; f32 group chance`. Native memory entries have a 32-byte name and a 44-byte stride. The shipped catalog has 16 entries; first `Beetle` kind 4 / mesh 36085, `Fish` kind 6 / mesh 36089, last `Pollen` kind 10. Visual meshes use **1010001** (`0xf6951`, dispatcher 0x1011a3c9). Dispatcher kind order 0..10: Bird, Rabbit, Butterfly, Fly, Beetle, TumbleWeed, Fish, Hoverbot, Trash, Cleanerbot, Pollen. District wildlife pairs index this catalog, not creature IDs (Newland district 1: `(0,5),(1,20),(11,10),(13,5),(2,1)`). Grouping is allowed only for kinds 0, 3, 6 (0x1011b90b); other kinds force the group chance to zero. Native on callback 0x1011bd03/flag 0x102c5b3c enters 0x1011bce4 → 0x1011bb4a; off 0x1011a394 → 0x1011a356 destroys every created Life Vehicle. Catalog/district decoding lives in `ao_formats::wildlife` / `ao_audio::district_reader`; native runtime/render lifecycle is tracked separately in the settings evidence table.

Wildlife runtime [CODE] (`play/native_life*.rs`): each catalog kind dispatches its own native steering/state machine, not a single generic flock. Bird `1011798c` switches flying/landing/perched/takeoff; Rabbit `1011d0be` switches idle/moving/stopping; Butterfly `10118330`, Fly `101198dc`, Fish `10118fc3`, Hoverbot `10119e57`, Beetle `10116ec7`, Cleanerbot `101189c3`, Trash `1011e693`, TumbleWeed `1011e9bc`, and Pollen `1011cace` retain their separate timers, water/terrain constraints, animation selection, and authored constructor motion limits. Species `1011d716` runs Vehicle only while visible, then `1011d88f` copies body position/rotation and advances the mesh animation. Vehicle `1000ce2f` sets maximum integration step **0.4 s** (`100127a0`), `1000e3d3` skips frames over **4 s**, uses gravity **−20** (`1001938c`), limits falling speed to **±50**, truncates steering force to the species maximum before mass integration, then limits velocity to its maximum. These are the base Vehicle constants, not the camera's 0.05 s override.

Wildlife surface integration [CODE, Vehicle `1000d1aa`, `1000e3d3`]: every placement and substep now uses the same `Collision::align` as character vehicles, retaining per-species `SurfaceState` (room veto, hover counter and liquid medium). Falling-enabled `+0x50` is distinct from airborne `+0x52`: only the latter integrates vertical gravity; support starts a fall or lands it and clears vertical speed. Surface-hug `+0x51` zeros steering Y only while supported. This replaces the wildlife-only ground-height clamp. The collision adapter mirrors Z once at its boundary; mode-1 body orientation retains its separate native three-ray normal sampling (`1000c616`, `1000f1f2`). Regression `native_support_state_controls_gravity_and_survives_substeps` covers supported rest, unsupported placement, gravity, landing and DisableFalling.

Wildlife liquid queries [CODE, N3 `1000cb1e`, `1001ab68`, DS `10039d3c`]: `GetLiquidSurfaceHeight(pos, ..., -1, -1)` first vetoes the position, then substitutes the query Y for the `-1` height sentinel (`1003ce48`). It is not an unbounded XZ lookup: the point must be below the liquid collision bounds and at or above its authored lower depth (`level - depth <= queryY`). Outdoor assembly `1001abdb–1001abeb` and dungeon `1001a847–1001a857` both use `FCOMPP; TEST AH,0x41; JP miss`, accepting equality as well as less-than. The first positive candidate decides even if its depth rejects the point. `Collision::liquid_surface_at` reuses `liquid_at`/`liquid_probe`, rather than a second polygon-only implementation; `wildlife_liquid_height_sentinel_uses_query_y_and_authored_depth` covers above-surface, inclusive lower-bound and overlapping-candidate cases.

Positional sound danger [CODE, SI `10002d98`, GC AFCM `100b29f1`]: positive authored scare radius (`record+0xdc`, sample `+0x280`) emits AFCM `9/0x119` at the source after the caller-volume gate, even if authored volume is zero or mixer allocation fails. The native gate compares attenuated caller volume and retained sample gain before multiplying authored volume; keepalive re-arms now preserve this order rather than testing the multiplied gain. Zero position remains non-positional and emits no sphere. `native_keepalive_scare_gate_precedes_authored_volume` covers silent definitions, retained gain, missing samples and non-positional calls; positive-duration effect playback preserves randomized authored gain without dividing by `vol_max`.

Wildlife CRT ownership [CODE, GC `1011bce4 → 1011bb4a`, DS `1001975e`]: wildlife enable reseeds the native thread's MSVCR100 stream with wall-clock seconds exactly once per disabled→enabled transition; constructors and steering consume that same stream, without independent per-species seeds or an extra seed draw. NPC variant/sound picks and Nano2 share `ao_scene::CrtRand::thread()`; Nano2's 42/time reseeds preserve the shared handle rather than replacing it with a local generator. `CrtRand::new(seed)` is an isolated deterministic fixture stream, and different threads retain separate native CRT state. Request regeneration clears old requests instead of appending them on re-enable. Regressions cover thread isolation/shared reseeding, interleaved species draws, idempotent enable and replacement of authored requests. Species constructor collision medium is **1** (`101173ba`, refuse deep water), except Fish **3** (`10118edb`, remain in liquid); placement also runs native orientation before the first substep.

Constructor CRT counters [CODE, Bird `101176cc`, Rabbit `1011cfc0`, Hoverbot `1011a239`, Cleanerbot `10118cce`] consume exactly one integer `rand() % 5`; they must not reconstruct the integer through normalized float division/multiplication. `native_constructor_counters_use_integer_crt_draws` covers 1024 explicit seeds for all four constructors and the following stream value.

Wildlife district manager [CODE] (`play/native_life_spawn.rs`): `Life_c::Run` **1011b627** activates authored zone requests around the **control dynel**, not the camera. Kind 0 (Bird) uses the linear 5×5 zone neighborhood; the other ten kinds use its inner 3×3. Visibility distance is recomputed every **5 s** (`102c5b40`), using horizontal squared distances **2916 / 729** (54 m / 27 m). Zone membership changes only when the control dynel changes zone. Native placement **1011b49c** makes at most ten authored-zone/water-triangle attempts per requested position; no rejected request becomes a fallback creature. Enable **1011bce4 → 1011bb4a** regenerates district requests and increments the cached center; disable **1011a394 → 1011a356** destroys spawned life immediately. Instance IDs stay distinct from catalog mesh IDs, and native animation segment clocks share immutable authored boundaries. The runnable `retail_authored_spawn_disable_and_reenable` regression loads district 566, actual referenced meshes and bounding-sphere/animation metadata, then checks instance destruction and fresh identities on re-enable.

LifeObject `+0x154/+0x158` are **lifespan/elapsed time**, not a braking force: `101194cb` increments elapsed when lifespan ≥ 0 and expires only when elapsed **strictly exceeds** lifespan; negative lifespan is unlimited. Beetle/Rabbit/Fish water escape writes a finite lifespan; `1011d716` writes zero lifespan on leaving the active zone neighborhood, and `Life::Run 1011b627` deletes expired objects after their tick. Steering accumulator `1011d9a6` consumes the remaining unit force budget in native priority order (0.99 early-return threshold); its return is consumed magnitude, not the length after opposing vectors cancel. Wander helpers `1011da78/1011dbc5` retain the species' evolving 3D/flat wander vector. Only Bird/Fly/Fish use authored groups, with native separation/cohesion/alignment radii and priority. `1011965c` filters neighbors by distance and velocity-relative angle; Fish's full-neighborhood helpers omit that angle filter.

Native danger spheres are a separate one-frame list (`100b8556`, producer `100b85dd`), not character bodies: VisualEnvFX kind 9 (`100af6f4`) and AFCM (`100b29f1`) append position/radius, and `1011b627` calls `100b85a3` after the wildlife pass to clear them. Character vicinity fleeing still queries type 50000 (`1011e401`); landing/corpse attraction (`1011e4f8`) uses the first type-50000 dynel with flag `+0x138` bit 4 in the same district, not corpse type 51050.

Vehicle orientation [CODE]: constructors set mode **0** for Bird/Rabbit/Butterfly/Fly/Fish/Hoverbot/Trash/Pollen and mode **1** only for Beetle/TumbleWeed/Cleanerbot. `1000c616` dispatches mode 0 to `1000f192`, which explicitly zeros forward Y (flat yaw even for flying/swimming models); stationary vehicles retain body forward. Mode 1 uses `1000f1f2`, projecting forward onto the upward surface plane. `EnsureSurfaceAlignment 1000d1aa` derives mode-1 normal from three rays along normalized XZ directions `(-1,0)`, `(1,1)`, `(1,-1)`, starting at `position + 0.4·Y + 0.2·direction` and ending at `position − 0.4·Y + 0.8·direction`; failed rays retain their endpoints. The upward triangle normal is smoothed as `normalize(0.9·previous + 0.1·current)` before orientation. Species animation immediate selection increments a request serial so selecting the already-playing segment still restarts it; queued selection does not restart until the current native segment ends. Event boundaries are shared immutable metadata across instances.

* **Zone lookup** (`ao_formats::playfield::zone_locator`, `ZoneLocator::zone_at`; `n3Playfield_t::GetZoneInstance` N3 @0x1000c97b [CODE]): outdoor — outside the map (`x < 0`, `z < 0`, `x > W·cell`, `z > H·cell`) is zone **0**, else `zone = floor(floor(z/cell)/zs)·(W/zs) + floor(floor(x/cell)/zs)` with `zs = zone_size` (10) and the tilemap size W×H (566: 150 × 150 cells of 4 m = 15 × 15 zones, matching its 225-zone district table). Dungeon — `PosToRoom` (@0x1000c8aa) takes the first room (spatial-grid candidates, which only prune; we test all rooms in list order) for which `n3Room_t::IsPosInside` (@0x10011664) holds; the **room index is the zone, none = 0**. `IsPosInside` (ported exactly in `zone.rs::room_contains`, verified: **all 270 dungeon entry spots of `spawn` lie inside a room**, test `dungeon_spawn_is_inside_its_room`): (1) x/z inside `GetRoomRect` (@0x100101cb) — `sx = (x2−x1)·2 m`, `sz = (z2−z1)·2 m` (`_DAT_1003c880`), extents swapped for odd `rot`, origin `pos − rotate(W'+1, H'+1)` with `W' = (((x2−x1)−1) & ~1) + 1` (`H'` likewise), rotation `x' = x cosθ + z sinθ, z' = −x sinθ + z cosθ` for `θ = rot·90°`, plus `+sx` on z (rot 1), `+sx` on x and `+sz` on z (rot 2), `+sz` on x (rot 3); both ends inclusive; (2) the tile under the point (`GetTilemapIndexFromPos` @0x10011077): inverse-rotate `pos − room.pos`, `u = floor((d + W' + 1) / 2)` per axis must lie in the room's `(x2−x1) × (z2−z1)` window, tile = atlas cell `(x1+ux, z1+uz)`, its `DCGA & 0x7f` index must be non-zero; (3) height window: `floorY = min(DHGA·hs over the 4 corner cells (tx,tz),(tx−1,tz),(tx−1,tz−1),(tx,tz−1), no −1 at atlas index 0) − room.minFloor + pos.y` (`minFloor` = lowest `DHGA·hs` of the room's non-empty tiles, `CalculateRoomHeights` @0x1001bd82), `v = tileHeight[DCGA & 0x7f]`, `s = stretch(HCDA)·hs`: inside when `floorY − 0.5 < y ≤ v·(1 + 0.25 s) + floorY + 0.5`. **`tileHeight` = the `HSTA` chunk of the GNDA blob** (`RDBTilemap_t::ReadBlob` DisplaySystem @0x10078391, the only writer of the float table at tilemap+0x8244): `u16 × n` (n = tile-id count of the header), `value / 10.0` m (double @DisplaySystem 0x10089ee8), indexed like `ids[]` (entry 0 = empty tile, 0xFFFF); **unlike every other GNDA section the `HSTA` size excludes its 12 byte header** (24 = 12 heights) and tilemaps with fewer than 6 tiles have `size < 12` (`Gnda::tile_height`).
* **Weather** (`ao_formats::weather`, [CODE] ported from Gamecode; unresolved parts below). Owner: `FUN_100bdb64` (sky manager ctor, per playfield) → area config (0x54 B, `FUN_100bc853`) filled from `EnvironmentData` by `FUN_100bd2c4`/`FUN_100bd183`: weights `w0..w7` = bytes 0..7 (`w > 1 ⇒ 2w + 100`, `FUN_100bce61`; Σw = `+0x40`, Σw = 0 ⇒ no weather ever. Measured through the code's own path (record → water → `EnvironmentData` → `weather::Config`, all 601 playfield records): **365 have Σw = 0, 22 only the clear weight (`w3`), 214 have a non-clear weight**; simulating `Weather::sample` over 6 game days × 12 times of day, **213 playfields get weather, 388 never do**, e.g. 505, 545, 550, 551, 565, 567, 600 besides 560, 590, 620, 625, 630, 635, 670, 695, 705, 710, 717, 790, 791; 566 has only `w3` = 100 → always clear), parameter bytes 8..0x12 (stream order swaps 0xa/0xb), colours 0x13..0x19.
  * **Schedule** (`FUN_100b12c9`): 3 windows of 6480 s (period 19439.998 s `_DAT_101683a0`, `/3.0` @0x101602d0), event gaps `rand·(1200−120)+120` (`_DAT_101683a8/a4`); window seeds `FUN_100bc8d1(−1/0/+1)` = Σ colour bytes + Σ parameter bytes + Σ weights + **game day** (`GameTime +0x4c`, server value `Update(.., day, ..)` @0x1000b526; offline `OFFLINE_DAY = 0`) + n (0 ⇒ 1). RNG = R250 `FUN_1013d8fa/93c/97c` (table @0x101717b8, `u32·2⁻³²` double @0x1016acb0), each event re-seeds the generator with its own word and stores 16 floats + 8 ints; draws are pre-increment ring reads (`FUN_100b0e34/e5e`). The schedule time is `fmod(realtime/15 + 6480, period)` (`FUN_100b0d1f/0fff`; `realtime = GameDayTime·15`): one game day covers only the middle window, the neighbours supply the previous event. Cursor `FUN_100b0d1f` (fires events with `last < t ≤ e`, wrap rule when `last > t`), slot 0 = previous event (`FUN_100b0f43`), slot 1 = current event (`FUN_100b0fa1`), `FUN_100bcfff` blends them with the ramp `elapsed/dur` (`dur = L·(0.25 + 0.75·draw)·(0.5 for storm bands)`, `FUN_100bc970` return value; `FUN_100bf417`, constants 0.25 @0x1015df60, 0.75 @0x10167e88) and recomputes only when its two frame-counted timers (`−1.0` per call, `+0x14/+0x18`) expire.
  * **Struct** (`FUN_100bc970`, per event `u = draw % Σw`): `u < w3` clear, else bands `w0` rain `f[0]` (draw), `w1` fog `f[1]` (draw·0.65), `w2` cloud `f[2]`, `w4..w7` sand storm / ash storm / fallout red / fallout green flags `f[3..=6] = 1`; then `f[7]` wind speed (`b8..b9`, ×2.75 @0x10168390, or `33·f[14]`), `f[8]` rain drops (`b10/b11`), `f[9]` lightning (needs `f[2] > 0.3`, `b12` %), `f[12]` quake rate (`b16/b17`), `f[2] = max(f[2], f[0], f[1], f[4], f[14])`, `f[15..=21]` = ambient, fog colour, fog density (constant colours). `f[14]` is only overwritten when a flag band is active, i.e. it keeps the last storm value in the struct (kept: reproduces the client bug). Names of `f[4]` (ash) are inferred from the debug-command order (`Env - Setting ash storm weather`), `f[3]/f[5]/f[6]` from the wind sound handles.
  * **Consumers**: `FUN_100be767` writes `ThickCloudsIntensity = f[2]`, `CloudTranspariency = 1 − f[2]/2.33333`, `GameStormIntensity = max f[3..=6]` (`+0xf0`), `GamePrecipitationIntensity = f[0]` (`+0xf4`) and the wind (see *Sky*); `FUN_100b6d67` (music, 1 Hz): `max f[3..=6] > 0.4` → slot 7, `f[0] > 0.4` → 6, `f[1] > 0.4` → 5 (`Audio::set_weather(State::music_state())`). Sound levels (`weather::Levels`, called every frame with a volume, `ao_audio::Audio::play_keepalive_level`): `SM_Sandy_Env_Rain` = `f[0]²·clamp(f[8]/3.5)`; wind object `FUN_100bfa11`: `base = (0.5 + 0.5·v/33)·v/33`, `v = clamp(f[7], 0.001, 33)` [INFERENCE: the length argument of `FUN_10023a81` is taken as the base wind speed] → `SM_Sandy_Env_Wind = base·max(1 − f[2], f[4])`, `SandWind = base·f[3]`, `FalloutRWind = base·f[5]`, `FalloutGWind = base·f[6]`; `SM_Sandy_Env_Quake` = time left / 2 s (`FUN_100be767` @0x100bed90: accumulator `rand%10000/100·dt/10·f[12]`, `≥ 100` fires, `FUN_100bd3ab`; seeded LCG here).
  * **Effect wind**: `FUN_100bd375` → `FUN_100bf699` copies the wind object's `+0xc/+0x10/+0x14` vector C (`base + A`, `FUN_100bf729`) into `_EffectHandler_t+0x24`, directly in AO XYZ coordinates, including vertical gusts. `ComputeWind` (`0x100cd993`, assembly `0x100cd9b1..0x100cd9ef`) sets handler `+0x30 = previous·0.96 + current·0.04` per frame (float constants `0x1016b2fc = 0x3f75c28f`, `0x1016b300 = 0x3d23d70a`), not a cloud-scroll-scaled vector or a delta-time-scaled velocity. `weather::State::wind` exposes C; the playfield loader supplies `Play::world_weather` independently of audio, updates it with the zone's server day/time each frame, and routes C through `Host::effect_wind` before dynel/effect drawing. The pre-frame wind state is zero; a weather load failure is a world-load error, not a fabricated calm-wind substitute.
  * **Lightning FX9 count/colour/lifetime [CODE]:** DisplaySystem `10065807` calls shared reset `10060935`, whose virtual+0x64 dispatch resolves through vtable `1008f2ec` to `10052182`. This is the missing count setter: `rand()%10+5` cross-sections (4..13 connections), each four vertices at fixed X/Z ±0.5, joined as two perpendicular strips. `10052182` writes BGRA `(255,255,200,A)`; first/last section A=0, interior A=255. `10051bca` initializes life to `rand()%500/1000` seconds, then draws X/Z jitter for **every** section, scaled by `t*(2-t)`, `t` advancing by `2/n`; this envelope is not strip-width taper and the last point need not equal the ground horizontally. `10051e5a` subtracts frame delta first, retires at life≤0, otherwise adds X/Z drift `(rand()%16000/1000-8)*t*(2-t)*dt`. Port: `ao_formats::lightning::Bolt`, crossed-strip actor skins in `play::lightning`.
